[SYSTEM]
You are an RTL (Register Transfer Level) design agent. Your task is to create a Verilog/SystemVerilog design that satisfies the given specification, is functionally correct, and has minimal cost (area_delay_product).

**Cost metric `area_delay_product`:** area × achieved critical-path delay — the classic area-delay product. Does NOT include simulation cycle count (contrast with the 'area_runtime_product' metric). Cost is measured on your design alone. The testbench and everything it owns (checking logic, the external memories, etc.) is not synthesized and contributes nothing to area, delay, power, or energy.

## Specification
Weight-stationary systolic matrix-multiply tile (a Google-TPU-style MXU),
module name `tpu`, implementing the EXACT per-cycle behavior of the logikbench
`tpu` benchmark (zeroasiccorp/logikbench, MIT).

FUNCTION: computes C = A * B tile products on an 8x8 grid of processing
elements. B (the "weight" matrix, 8-bit signed) is loaded into the array and
held; A (the "activation" matrix, 8-bit signed) streams through; result rows
C (32-bit signed) drain out the bottom.

PROTOCOL:
  1. Weight load: assert w_valid for 8 cycles; w_data carries one weight row
     per cycle, 8 signed bytes packed { col 7 ... col 0 }, BOTTOM matrix row
     first (cycle k carries B[7-k][*]; the weights shift south). After 8
     cycles PE(i,j) holds B[i][j].
  2. Compute: assert a_valid while streaming A one row per cycle (packed the
     same way). Row m of the result, C[m][*] packed { col 7 ... col 0 } as 8
     signed 32-bit words, appears on c_data with c_valid high exactly 15
     (= 2*8-1) compute cycles after its A row was accepted.
  - The array advances only while w_valid is low: asserting w_valid stalls
    any in-flight computation (and reloads weights). Zeros are injected while
    a_valid is low, so idle/drain cycles add nothing to the accumulators.
  - Multiple A tiles may stream back-to-back against the same loaded weights.

CYCLE ACCURACY: c_valid is compared against the original design EVERY cycle;
c_data is compared on every cycle where c_valid is high. The 15-cycle
compute-latency and the stall semantics are part of the contract.

Ports:
  clk, rst (async active-high reset, asserted only before the stream begins)
  w_valid, w_data[63:0]
  a_valid, a_data[63:0]
  c_valid, c_data[255:0]

A working starting point is provided: `starting_point.v` is the
original logikbench RTL (tpu_pe / tpu_array / tpu: PE mesh, per-row input
skew, per-column output de-skew, valid pipeline). It passes every cycle of the golden
trace in `vectors.dat` (directed zero/identity/extreme tiles, random tiles,
back-to-back streams, a_valid gaps, mid-drain weight reloads, random soak).
Study it, then optimize while preserving the cycle-accurate behavior. The
sibling benchmark `tpu_spire` shares the identical testbench and vectors
with a SpireHDL starting point.

## Tools
- create_file: Create a new file in your working directory
- replace_file: Replace the entire contents of an existing file
- apply_diff: Apply a unified diff to modify a file
- edit_file: Edit a file by replacing an exact string match with new content. Provide old_string (must match exactly once) and new_string.
- ls: List files in your working directory
- read_file: Read the contents of a file
- run_evaluation(filename[, target_delay]): Run evaluation on the given design file. Returns correctness (Verilator simulation) + cost (Yosys area_delay_product). The filename must be your main design file (e.g. 'design.sv'). Optionally pass target_delay (ps) to override the synthesis timing constraint.

## Strategy
1. Lay out an action plan. Try to cover a diverse set of approaches in your plan to increase the chances of finding a good solution within the step limit. Also once you find a new best solution, explore close solutions. Trade off exploration with exploitation.
2. First, create a simple, straightforward design that is functionally correct. Focus on getting 100% correctness first.
3. Run evaluation to confirm correctness and get the initial cost (area_delay_product).
4. ONLY after achieving 100% correctness, try to optimize the design to reduce the area_delay_product.
5. After each optimization, run evaluation to verify correctness is maintained and check the new cost.
6. If an optimization breaks correctness, revert and try a different approach.
7. Keep iterating until you run out of steps — use every step to try improvements. The run ends automatically at the step limit; your best passing design is kept.

In general, be creative and take risk. At least after you have a correct design, try bold optimizations to reduce area_delay_product, even if they might break correctness. You can always revert if it doesn't work.
The best results you obtain which is 100% correctness counts.
Try to beat at least the straightforward implementation.

Running evaluation is crucial after every change to ensure correctness and track cost. Do not skip evaluations.

You may do some 'thinking' alongside with your tool calling responses to explain your reasoning, but always remember to call a tool in every response.

## Important
- Always call a tool in every response.
- You have a maximum of **60 steps** (LLM turns). Budget them wisely: get a correct design first, then optimise. You may batch tool calls in one response --- at most 8, and only one `run_evaluation`.
- The best result is the one with lowest cost among designs that pass 100% of testbench checks.
- Use different filenames for different design variants so you can retrieve them later.
- If context files (e.g. component source files) are in your workspace, you can modify them directly using edit_file or replace_file to optimize the design. Make targeted, incremental changes rather than rewriting files from scratch — small edits are less likely to introduce bugs.
- Write clean, synthesizable RTL code.
- You can pass `target_delay` (ps) to `run_evaluation` to try different synthesis timing constraints; experimenting with this may improve PPA results (lower target delay may reduce delay, higher target delay may reduce area/transistor count). target_delay must be > 0.
- Do not spend too many steps sweeping `target_delay` — basically if you want to minimize delay choose a low value,if you want to minimize area/transistor count choose a high value. That's it.A few targeted experiments are more productive than an exhaustive sweep.

## Delay Optimization Tips
- `target_delay` trades area against delay (a lower value pressures toward faster logic, a higher value lets Yosys re-map to smaller cells). Do not overly iterate on different target delays; often the RTL structure is the real lever, target_delay is only a minor secondary knob.
- After getting a correct design, try different accumulation orderings (sequential vs. tree) and compare results — Yosys synthesis can produce noticeably different delays (~10 ps) from structurally similar descriptions.
- For designs dominated by a multiply-accumulate chain, the critical path is the multiplier followed by the adder tree. Reducing the adder depth or splitting into parallel partial sums can help.

## Reference Implementations
The following are reference implementations for inspiration. Study them for patterns and techniques, but do not copy them directly — adapt ideas to your specific design.

### Parallel-prefix adder (ripple-carry / carry-lookahead) (Add.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Binary adder using ripple-carry or parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.
// S = A+B

module Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);

	// Function: Binary adder using parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.

	logic [width-1:0] GI, PI;  // prefix gen./prop. in
	logic [width-1:0] GO, PO;  // prefix gen./prop. out
	logic [width-1:0] PT;  // adder propagate temp

	// Internal signals for unsigned operands
	logic [width-1:0] Auns, Buns, Suns;

	// default ripple-carry adder as slow implementation
	if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin
		// type conversion: std_logic_vector -> unsigned
		assign Auns = A;
		assign Buns = B;

		// addition
		assign Suns = Auns + Buns;

		// type conversion: unsigned -> std_logic_vector
		assign S = Suns;
	end else begin
		// parallel-prefix adders as medium and fast implementations

		// calculate prefix input generate/propagate signals
		assign GI = A & B;
		assign PI = A | B;
		// calculate adder propagate signals (PT = A xor B)
		assign PT = ~GI & PI;

		// calculate prefix output generate/propagate signals
		PrefixAndOr #(
			.width(width),
			.speed(speed)
		) prefix (
			.GI(GI),
			.PI(PI),
			.GO(GO),
			.PO(PO)
		);

		// calculate sum bits
		assign S = PT ^ {GO[width-2:0], 1'b0};
	end
endmodule



module behavioural_Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);
	assign S = A + B;
endmodule
```

### Prefix AND-OR structures (Sklansky, Brent-Kung, serial) (PrefixAndOr.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Prefix structures of different depth (i.e. speed) for carry calculation
// in binary adders. Compute in m levels new generate/propagate signal pairs
// for always larger groups of bits. Generate signals of the last level correspond
// to carries in binary addition. Basic logic operations: AND-OR for generate
// signals, AND for propagate signals.

module PrefixAndOr #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] GI,  // gen./prop. in
	input  logic [width-1:0] PI,  // gen./prop. in
	output logic [width-1:0] GO,  // gen./prop. out
	output logic [width-1:0] PO   // gen./prop. out
);

	// Constants
	localparam int n = width;  // prefix structure width
	localparam int m = $clog2(width);  // prefix structure depth

	// Sklansky parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::FAST) begin : fastPrefix
		logic [(m+1)*n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
			assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
			assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;
			for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels
				for (genvar k = 0; k < 2 ** (m - l); k++) begin : groups
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2 ** (l - 1); i++) begin : bits
						// pass prop and gen to following nodes
						if ((k * 2 ** l + i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = GT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
							assign PT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = PT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
						end
						// calculate new propagate and generate
						if ((k * 2 ** l + 2 ** (l - 1) + i) < n) begin : black
							assign GT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
												GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
											  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												 & GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1] );
							assign PT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
													PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1];
						end
					end
				end
			end
			assign GO = GT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
			assign PO = PT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
	end

	// Brent-Kung parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::MEDIUM) begin : mediumPrefix
		logic [(2*m)*n -1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
		assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;

		for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels1
			for (genvar k = 0; k < 2**(m-l); k++) begin : groups
				for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**l -1; i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**l +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
					end // white
				end // bits
				if ((k* 2**l + 2**l -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l - 1] |
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							     & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**(l-1) -1]);
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) -1];
				end // black
			end
		end // level1
		for (genvar l = m +1; l < 2*m; l++) begin : levels2
			for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
				if (i < n) begin : white
					assign GT[l*n +i] = GT[(l-1)*n +i];
					assign PT[l*n +i] = PT[(l-1)*n +i];
				end // white
			end // bits
			for (genvar k = 1; k < 2**(l-m); k++) begin : groups
				if (l < 2*m -1) begin : empty
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l -1) -1; i++) begin : bits
						if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
							assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						end // white
					end
				end // empty
				if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l -1) -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m-l-1) -1] 
								 & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) -1] );
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) -1];
				end // black
				for (genvar i = 2**(2*m -l-1); i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
					end // white
				end
			end
		end // level2
		assign GO = GT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
		assign PO = PT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
	end  // Serial-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	else if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin : slowPrefix
		logic [n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[0] = GI[0];
		assign PT[0] = PI[0];
		
		for (genvar i = 1; i < n; i++) begin : bits
			assign GT[i] = GI[i] | (PI[i] & GT[i-1]);
			assign PT[i] = PI[i] & PT[i-1];
		end
		assign GO = GT;
		assign PO = PT;
	end

endmodule

```

[Step 1/60]
//#############################################################################
// Copyright: Zero ASIC. All rights Reserved.
// Author: Andreas Olofsson
// License:  MIT (see LICENSE file in LogikBench repository)
//#############################################################################
//
// Processing element (PE) for the weight-stationary systolic array (TPU MXU).
//
// Each PE holds one stationary signed weight 'w'. Two independent datapaths
// share the cell:
//
//   Weight load (ld): the weights form a per-column shift register that loads
//   from the top. On each ld cycle the PE captures the weight from the PE above
//   (w_north) and presents its stored weight downward (w_south). After N load
//   cycles every PE in a column holds its final stationary weight.
//
//   Compute (en): the activation flows west to east (registered, one hop per
//   cycle) and the partial sum flows north to south, accumulating the local
//   product a_west * w. This is the only arithmetic cell in the array: one
//   signed DW x DW multiply plus an ACCW-bit add.
//
//#############################################################################

module tpu_pe
  #(parameter DW = 8,	 // operand width (signed)
    parameter ACCW = 32) // accumulator / partial-sum width (signed)
   (
    input		  clk,
    input		  rst,	      // synchronous, active high
    input		  ld,	      // weight-load shift enable
    input		  en,	      // compute enable
    input [DW-1:0]	  w_north,    // weight in from PE above
    input [DW-1:0]	  a_west,     // activation in from west
    input [ACCW-1:0]	  psum_north, // partial sum in from above
    output [DW-1:0]	  w_south,    // stored weight out to PE below
    output reg [DW-1:0]	  a_east,     // activation out to east
    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south  // partial sum out to below
    );

   // stationary weight register; presented downward for the load shift chain
   reg [DW-1:0] w;
   assign w_south = w;

   // local product: the ONLY signed operation. Operands are interpreted as
   // signed here via $signed; the 2*DW-bit result holds the two's-complement
   // product, which is then sign-extended to the accumulator width below.
   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);

   always @(posedge clk) begin
      if (rst) begin
         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};
         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};
         psum_south <= {ACCW{1'b0}};
      end
      else begin
         if (ld)
           w <= w_north;
         if (en) begin
            a_east     <= a_west;
            psum_south <= psum_north +
                          {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};
         end
      end
   end

endmodule
//#############################################################################
// Copyright: Zero ASIC. All rights Reserved.
// Author: Andreas Olofsson
// License:  MIT (see LICENSE file in LogikBench repository)
//#############################################################################
//
// N x N weight-stationary systolic array (the TPU MXU core).
//
// One tpu_pe per grid element, wired as a 2D mesh (per CLAUDE.md: replicated
// hardware via generate, never a procedural unroll):
//
//   - weights load from the top: w_top[j] feeds column j; each column is a
//     vertical shift register (w_south of PE(i,j) -> w_north of PE(i+1,j)).
//   - activations flow west to east: a_left[i] enters row i; a_east of PE(i,j)
//     feeds a_west of PE(i,j+1).
//   - partial sums flow north to south and accumulate; the top row's psum_north
//     is tied to zero (fresh accumulation), and c_bot[j] is the column-j result
//     leaving the bottom row.
//
// With weight B[i][j] held at PE(i,j) and activation A[m][i] entering row i,
// column j computes C[m][j] = sum_i A[m][i] * B[i][j]. Input/output skew (which
// time-aligns rows and columns) lives in the tpu top, not here.
//
//#############################################################################

module tpu_array
  #(parameter N = 8,	 // array dimension (N x N PEs)
    parameter DW = 8,	 // operand width (signed)
    parameter ACCW = 32) // accumulator width (signed)
   (
    input		clk,
    input		rst,	// synchronous, active high
    input		ld,	// weight-load shift enable
    input		en,	// compute enable
    input [N*DW-1:0]	w_top,	// weight inputs, one per column (load)
    input [N*DW-1:0]	a_left,	// activation inputs, one per row
    output [N*ACCW-1:0]	c_bot	// column results, one per column
    );

   // mesh nets: a_h carries activations east, psum_v carries sums south,
   // w_v carries weights south during load.
   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];
   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];
   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];

   genvar	   i, j;
   generate
      // west edge: activation inputs into column 0 of each row
      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west
         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];
      end
      // top edge: zero partial sums, weight inputs; bottom edge: results out

      // top edge: zero partial sums, weight inputs; bottom edge: results out
      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge

         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];

      end

      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row
         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col
            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe

               .rst        (rst),

               .en         (en),

               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),

               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),
               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),
               .psum_south (psum_v[i+1][j]));
         end
      end
   endgenerate

endmodule
//#############################################################################
// Copyright: Zero ASIC. All rights Reserved.
// Author: Andreas Olofsson
// License:  MIT (see LICENSE file in LogikBench repository)
//#############################################################################
//
// Weight-stationary systolic matrix-multiply tile (a Google-TPU-style MXU).
//
// Computes one tile product C = A * B, where A and B are N x N signed operands
// (DW bits) and C is N x N signed (ACCW bits). B is the "weight" matrix, loaded
// into and held by the array; A is the "activation" matrix, streamed through; C
// drains out the bottom. Built from a tpu_array of N x N tpu_pe cells.
//
// Operation has two phases the host drives via valids:
//
//   1. Weight load: assert w_valid for N cycles. w_data carries one weight row
//      per cycle, N signed weights packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }. Because the
//      array loads top-down (shift south), push the BOTTOM matrix row first:
//      cycle k carries B[N-1-k][*]. After N cycles PE(i,j) holds B[i][j].
//
//   2. Compute: assert a_valid while streaming A one row per cycle, a_data
//      packed { col N-1 ... col 0 } = A[m][N-1..0]. Row m of the result, C[m][*]
//      (packed the same way), appears on c_data with c_valid high, 2*N-1 cycles
//      after its A row was accepted.
//
// Time alignment: row i of A is delayed i cycles entering the array (input
// skew) so partial sums chain correctly down each column; column j of the
// result is delayed N-1-j cycles (output de-skew) so a full result row leaves
// together. Both skew networks and the valid pipeline advance only with the
// array (compute enable), so the 2*N-1 latency is exact. Per CLAUDE.md the skew
// chains are generated per-row/column shift registers, not a procedural unroll.
//
//#############################################################################

module tpu
  #(parameter N = 8,	 // array dimension (N x N)
    parameter DW = 8,	 // operand width (signed)
    parameter ACCW = 32) // result / accumulator width (signed)
   (
    input		clk,
    input		rst,	 // synchronous, active high
    input		w_valid, // weight-row valid (load phase)
    input [N*DW-1:0]	w_data,	 // weight row, packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }
    input		a_valid, // activation-row valid (compute phase)
    input [N*DW-1:0]	a_data,	 // activation row, packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }
    output		c_valid, // result-row valid
    output [N*ACCW-1:0]	c_data	 // result row, packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }
    );

   // total injection-to-output latency, in compute cycles
   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;

   // load weights while w_valid; otherwise the array computes (and drains)
   wire	      ld = w_valid;
   wire	      en = ~w_valid;

   //##########################################################################
   // Input skew: row i of the activation is delayed i cycles. Zero is injected
   // when a_valid is low so idle/drain cycles add nothing to the accumulators.
   //##########################################################################
   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;
   genvar	   i;
   generate
      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew
         wire [DW-1:0] din =
		       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};
         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0
            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;
         end
         else begin : g_skewn
            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];
            integer k;
            always @(posedge clk) begin
               if (rst) begin
                  for (k = 0; k < i; k = k + 1)
                    sr[k] <= {DW{1'b0}};
               end
               else if (en) begin
                  sr[0] <= din;
                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)
                    sr[k] <= sr[k-1];
               end
            end
            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = sr[i-1];
         end
      end
   endgenerate

   //##########################################################################
   // The systolic array.
   //##########################################################################
   wire [N*ACCW-1:0] c_bot;
   tpu_array #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_array
     (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .ld(ld), .en(en),
      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), .c_bot(c_bot));

   //##########################################################################
   // Output de-skew: column j is delayed N-1-j cycles so all columns of a
   // result row line up. Column N-1 (longest path) needs no delay.
   //##########################################################################
   genvar j;
   generate
      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_deskew
         localparam D = N - 1 - j;
         wire [ACCW-1:0] cin = c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW];
         if (D == 0) begin : g_deskew0
            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = cin;
         end
         else begin : g_deskewn
            reg [ACCW-1:0] dr [0:D-1];
            integer k;
            always @(posedge clk) begin
               if (rst) begin
                  for (k = 0; k < D; k = k + 1)
                    dr[k] <= {ACCW{1'b0}};
               end
               else if (en) begin
                  dr[0] <= cin;
                  for (k = 1; k < D; k = k + 1)
                    dr[k] <= dr[k-1];
               end
            end
            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = dr[D-1];
         end
      end
   endgenerate

   //##########################################################################
   // Valid pipeline: c_valid is a_valid delayed LAT compute cycles.
   //##########################################################################
   reg [LAT-1:0] vpipe;
   always @(posedge clk) begin
      if (rst)
        vpipe <= {LAT{1'b0}};
      else if (en)
        vpipe <= {vpipe[LAT-2:0], a_valid};
   end
   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];

endmodule

// Self-checking testbench for the tpu benchmark (8x8 weight-stationary
// systolic matmul tile). Reads vectors.dat, one line per clock cycle:
//   w_valid w_data(64b) a_valid a_data(64b)   c_valid c_data(256b)   (hex)
// The expected outputs are the ORIGINAL logikbench tpu's per-cycle behavior;
// c_valid and c_data are compared CYCLE-ACCURATELY every cycle (c_data only
// while c_valid is high — the original holds stale data between results).
module tb;
  localparam N = 8, DW = 8, ACCW = 32;
  int total_checks;
  int total_errors;

  logic clk, rst;
  logic w_valid, a_valid, c_valid;
  logic [N*DW-1:0] w_data, a_data;
  logic [N*ACCW-1:0] c_data;

  tpu dut
    (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .w_valid(w_valid), .w_data(w_data),
     .a_valid(a_valid), .a_data(a_data), .c_valid(c_valid), .c_data(c_data));

  initial clk = 0;
  always #5 clk = ~clk;

  integer fd, rc, line_num;
  string line_buf;
  logic s_wv, s_av, e_cv;
  logic [63:0] s_wd, s_ad;
  logic [255:0] e_cd;

  initial begin
    total_checks = 0;
    total_errors = 0;
    w_valid = 0; a_valid = 0; w_data = 0; a_data = 0;
    rst = 1;
    repeat (4) @(negedge clk);
    rst = 0;

    fd = $fopen("vectors.dat", "r");
    if (fd == 0) begin
      $display("ERROR: cannot open vectors.dat");
      $fatal(1);
    end
    line_num = 0;
    while (!$feof(fd)) begin
      line_num = line_num + 1;
      void'($fgets(line_buf, fd));
      if (line_buf.len() == 0) continue;
      if (line_buf.substr(0, 0) == "#") continue;
      rc = $sscanf(line_buf, "%h %h %h %h %h %h", s_wv, s_wd, s_av, s_ad, e_cv, e_cd);
      if (rc != 6) continue;
      w_valid = s_wv; w_data = s_wd; a_valid = s_av; a_data = s_ad;
      @(negedge clk);
      total_checks = total_checks + 1;
      if (c_valid !== e_cv || (e_cv && c_data !== e_cd)) begin
        $display("TB_ERROR line=%0d exp(cv=%b cd=%h) got(cv=%b cd=%h)",
                 line_num, e_cv, e_cd, c_valid, c_data);
        total_errors = total_errors + 1;
        if (total_errors > 20) begin
          $display("TB_SUMMARY total=%0d errors=%0d", total_checks, total_errors);
          $fatal(1, "FAIL (too many errors, aborting)");
        end
      end
    end
    $fclose(fd);

    $display("TB_SUMMARY total=%0d errors=%0d", total_checks, total_errors);
    if (total_errors != 0) $fatal(1, "FAIL");
    $display("PASS");
    $finish;
  end
endmodule

[ASSISTANT]
Baseline: cost = 4,624,408, delay = 1004ps, area = 4604. The critical path is through the psum chain (8 multiplies + adds cascaded). The target_delay is 500ps but achieved delay is 1004ps.

Let me think about optimization strategies:

1. **The psum chain is the critical path**: In each PE, `psum_south = psum_north + product`. This is a chain of 8 adds through the column. But wait — in the actual implementation, the psum is *registered* in each PE, so the critical path is just one multiply + one add per cycle, not the full chain. Looking at the timing path more carefully, it goes through the weight register → multiplier → adder → deskew chain.

Let me look at the path: it starts from `w` register (weight), goes through what appears to be a multiplier and adder chain. The path is 1004ps which is very long. This suggests Yosys is implementing the 8x8 multiply + 32-bit add combination inefficiently.

Let me try a key optimization: **separate the multiply and accumulate into pipeline stages**. But I can't add latency since the design is cycle-accurate. 

Actually, looking more carefully — the critical path starts from the weight register `w` (which feeds the multiplier), goes through the multiplier, and then through the adder. The product `$signed(a_west) * $signed(w)` combined with the `psum_north +` addition creates a long combinational path: multiply + add in one cycle.

One approach: precompute the product in a separate register. But that changes timing. Let me think about what I can do without changing cycle behavior.

Actually, the key insight is: `a_west` is already registered (it comes from `a_east` of the previous PE). And `w` is registered. So the multiplier inputs are both registered. The output of the multiplier goes through the adder to `psum_south` register. The critical path is: **registered a_west × registered w → 32-bit add → psum_south register**.

Let me try optimizing the multiply+add implementation. One approach is to use a Booth multiplier or to structure the arithmetic differently. Let me also try different target_delay values.

Let me first try a higher target_delay to reduce area, and a lower one to see if delay can improve:
  -> run_evaluation(filename='design.sv', target_delay=1500)

Let me now try a fundamentally different approach to reduce the critical path. The key insight: the critical path goes through `w` (weight register) → multiply → add. If I can break this path, I can reduce delay.

However, I notice that `a_west` is already registered in the previous PE's `a_east`. The weight `w` is stationary. So I could register the product output... but that changes the timing.

- `a_east <= a_west` (register activation)
- `psum_south <= psum_north + product` (accumulate)

One approach to reduce delay: **pre-register the product**. But that adds a cycle. The latency budget is fixed at 15 cycles (2*8-1).

Actually, let me reconsider. The latency is: input skew (0 to 7 cycles) + PE column traversal (0 to 7 cycles for activation) + PE row traversal (0 to 7 for psum) + output deskew (0 to 7 cycles). The total for each element is exactly 14 (2*N-2) or 15 depending on exact counting.

Let me think about this more carefully. The latency structure is:
- Row i of A enters at cycle t+i (input skew)
- Activation takes j cycles to traverse to PE(i,j)
- Psum takes N-1-i cycles to traverse from PE(i,j) down to the bottom
- Then deskew adds N-1-j cycles

Total from A row m entry to C row m output: i + j + (N-1-i) + (N-1-j) = 2N-2 = 14 cycles after the row enters... plus 1 for the first cycle = 15 = LAT.

Actually wait, the latency is measured from when a_valid is asserted (A row m accepted) to when c_valid goes high for C row m. The input skew means row i is delayed by i cycles. The total is 2*N-1 = 15.

Interesting - with target_delay=2000, the delay is 1085ps and area is 4575. The delay is actually *worse* than at 500ps (1004ps) but area is slightly better. The minimum delay achievable with the current RTL is about 1004ps.

Now let me think about the carry-save accumulation approach more carefully. This is the most promising optimization.

**Carry-save accumulation concept:**
- Instead of `psum_south = psum_north + product` (full propagate add), use:
  - `{sum_south, carry_south} = CSA(psum_north_sum, psum_north_carry, product)`
  - Each PE outputs two vectors: sum and carry
  - The final PE (or bottom of column) does a propagate add to merge carry-save
- The CSA (3:2 compressor) is much faster than a full adder
- The final propagate add at the bottom can be done in the deskew stage

The timing challenge: the final propagate add adds 1 cycle. But the deskew chain already has variable delays. Let me think...

Actually, the propagate add at the bottom would be in the same cycle as the last CSA. If I make the bottom of each column do a propagate add in a registered stage, that adds 1 cycle to the latency. The original deskew for column j is N-1-j cycles. If I add 1 cycle to all columns, the total latency becomes 2*N, not 2*N-1. That breaks the contract.

Alternatively, I could do the final propagate add *combinationally* at the bottom, without a register. Then the critical path would be CSA_chain(registered) → propagate_add → deskew_register. But CSA is registered, so the path is just: last CSA register → propagate_add → deskew_register. The propagate add is a 32-bit add, which is about the same as the current critical path.

Hmm, that might not help. Let me think differently.

- Stage 1: multiply → product register (fast multiply path)
- Stage 2: product_register + psum_north → psum_south_register (fast add path)

This adds 1 cycle to the psum chain. The psum chain has N=8 PEs. But actually, the product register stage means that when the activation enters PE(i,j), the product is ready one cycle later. So the psum from PE(i-1,j) arrives and gets added to the product at the same time.

Wait, let me think about the timing more carefully:

Current PE:
- Cycle T: a_west arrives (registered), psum_north arrives (registered)
- Combinational: product = a_west * w; psum_south = psum_north + product
- Cycle T+1: psum_south is registered, a_east is registered

- Cycle T: a_west arrives, psum_north arrives

- Cycle T+1: product_reg available, psum_north_reg available

So this adds 1 cycle per PE in the column. The psum column has N=8 PEs, so the total extra latency is 8 cycles. That's way too much.

Let me think about this differently. The total latency for column j is:
- Input skew for row i: i cycles (delay entering row i)
- Activation traversal: j cycles (from column 0 to column j)
- Psum traversal: N-1-i cycles (from row i to row N-1, but now with extra register, N-1-i + (N-i) = 2N-1-2i... no, it's not that simple)

Actually, the psum flows south. PE(i,j) produces psum that goes to PE(i+1,j). With the extra product register, each PE takes 2 cycles instead of 1 to pass the psum through. So the psum path from PE(0,j) to PE(N-1,j) takes 2*(N-1) cycles instead of N-1 cycles.

The original latency for column j is: skew(i) + j (activation to column) + (N-1-i) (psum down) + (N-1-j) (deskew) = i + j + N-1-i + N-1-j = 2N-2. Plus 1 for the pipeline register = 2N-1 = 15.

With the extra register: psum path becomes 2*(N-1-i) instead of N-1-i. So total = i + j + 2*(N-1-i) + deskew = i + j + 2N-2-2i + deskew = j - i + 2N-2 + deskew. For this to equal 2N-1 for all (i,j), we need j - i + deskew = 1, so deskew = 1 - j + i. But deskew can't depend on i (it's per-column). So this doesn't work uniformly.

This approach won't preserve cycle accuracy easily. Let me try a different strategy.

**Strategy: Optimize the existing multiply+add by using a carry-save adder within the PE itself.** The idea is to compute `psum_north + product` using a carry-save adder that's faster than a ripple-carry adder. Yosys should already be doing this, but maybe I can help it.

  -> create_file(content="// Carry-save accumulation TPU\n// PEs keep psum in carry-save form (sum, carry), CSA instead of full add\n// Final propagate add at bottom of each column, absorbed into deskew\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north_s,  // carry-save sum in\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north_c,  // carry-save carry in\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south_s, // carry-save sum out\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south_c  // carry-save carry out\n   );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n\n   // Carry-save add: sum = a ^ b ^ c, carry = (a&b) | (a&c) | (b&c)\n   // Here a = psum_north_s, b = psum_north_c, c = prod_ext\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] cs_sum = psum_north_s ^ psum_north_c ^ prod_ext;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] cs_carry = (psum_north_s & psum_north_c) |\n                              (psum_north_s & prod_ext) |\n                              (psum_north_c & prod_ext);\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_south_s <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         psum_south_c <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east     <= a_west;\n            psum_south_s <= cs_sum;\n            psum_south_c <= {cs_carry[ACCW-2:0], 1'b0}; // carry shifted left\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_s_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_c_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_s_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign psum_c_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         // Final propagate add at the bottom: merge carry-save\n         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = psum_s_v[N][j] + psum_c_v[N][j];\n      end\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .rst        (rst),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_north_s (psum_s_v[i][j]),\n               .psum_north_c (psum_c_v[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_south_s (psum_s_v[i+1][j]),\n               .psum_south_c (psum_c_v[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew\n         wire [DW-1:0] din =\n\t\t       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;\n         end\n         else begin : g_skewn\n            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= {DW{1'b0}};\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  sr[0] <= din;\n                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= sr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = sr[i-1];\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   wire [N*ACCW-1:0] c_bot;\n   tpu_array #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_array\n     (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .ld(ld), .en(en),\n      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), .c_bot(c_bot));\n\n   genvar j;\n   generate\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_deskew\n         localparam D = N - 1 - j;\n         wire [ACCW-1:0] cin = c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW];\n         if (D == 0) begin : g_deskew0\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = cin;\n         end\n         else begin : g_deskewn\n            reg [ACCW-1:0] dr [0:D-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; k < D; k = k + 1)\n                    dr[k] <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  dr[0] <= cin;\n                  for (k = 1; k < D; k = k + 1)\n                    dr[k] <= dr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = dr[D-1];\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   reg [LAT-1:0] vpipe;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst)\n        vpipe <= {LAT{1'b0}};\n      else if (en)\n        vpipe <= {vpipe[LAT-2:0], a_valid};\n   end\n   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];\n\nendmodule\n", filename='design_cs.sv')

Excellent! The carry-save approach works and is a huge improvement! Cost dropped from 4,624,408 to 3,104,542 — a 33% reduction! Delay dropped from 1004ps to 679ps, area roughly the same (4568 vs 4604).

Let me try to optimize further. The critical path now goes through the bottom propagate add. Let me try to reduce this:

[ASSISTANT]
Same delay at 400ps target — Yosys can't make it faster. The critical path is: weight reg → 8×8 multiply (partial product tree) → CSA (MAJ gates) → final 32-bit propagate add → deskew register.

Looking at the path breakdown:
- Multiply partial products: ~370ps (XOR/XNOR chain)
- CSA (MAJ gates): ~70ps
- Final propagate add: ~240ps (O2A1O1I, AOI211, etc.)

The multiply and the final propagate add are both significant. Let me try to optimize the final propagate add. Since we're doing carry-save, the final add is a 32-bit addition. Let me try making this a registered stage instead of combinational, which would split the path.

If I register the propagate add output, I add 1 cycle. But I can reduce the deskew by 1 cycle to compensate! The deskew for column j is N-1-j. If I change it to N-2-j, the total latency stays the same.

Wait, but column N-1 currently has 0 deskew (D=0). If I subtract 1, it becomes -1, which doesn't make sense. Column N-1 needs 0 deskew because it's the slowest. With a registered propagate add, column N-1 would need -1 deskew, meaning it needs to be faster, which is impossible.

Unless... the propagate add is only at the bottom of the column, and for column N-1, the path is: last CSA reg → propagate_add → c_data. If I register the propagate add, the path becomes: last CSA reg → propagate_add → reg, and then reg → c_data. This adds 1 cycle for ALL columns. Column N-1 would then need deskew of -1 to compensate, which is impossible.

So registering the propagate add doesn't work directly. But what if I use a different deskew assignment? What if the bottom propagate add is done in a way that's fast enough?

Let me try another approach: **move the propagate add into the bottom PE itself**. Instead of a separate combinational add after the array, have the bottom row of PEs do the final addition internally. The bottom PE would do: CSA → register → propagate_add → register. But that's 2 cycles for the bottom PE instead of 1.

Actually, let me try a different optimization: **reduce the multiply delay by using a Booth-encoded or partial-product-tree multiplier**. Or try to restructure the multiply to use fewer levels of logic.

Let me try yet another idea: instead of the full 8×8 multiply in each PE, use the fact that the weight is stationary and precompute partial products of the weight. During weight load, precompute `w * 0..255` lookup... no, that's too much.

Let me try: instead of `$signed(a_west) * $signed(w)`, explicitly decompose into partial products using AND gates and a Wallace tree:
  -> create_file(content="// Carry-save accumulation TPU with optimized propagate add placement\n// Try: move propagate add into bottom PE, absorbed as combinational\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_s_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_c_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] Actually, the propagate add is `psum_s + psum_c`. Since `psum_c` has its LSB forced to 0 (I shift carry left), the LSB of the sum is just `psum_s[0]`. And the carry from bit 0 is always 0. So the add is slightly simpler.

Actually, let me try a completely different strategy. Instead of carry-save through the psum chain, let me try **pre-registering the product** and reducing the deskew by 1 to compensate.

The idea: PE does:
- `a_east <= a_west` (register activation)

Wait, this doesn't work because `prod_reg_prev` is the product from the *previous* cycle's activation, but `psum_north` is the partial sum that corresponds to the *current* activation's column traversal. The timing would be off.

Let me think again. In the original design:
- Cycle T: activation enters PE(i,j) via a_west (registered from PE(i,j-1))
- Cycle T: psum arrives from PE(i-1,j) via psum_north (registered)
- Combinational: product = a_west * w; psum_south = psum_north + product
- Cycle T+1: psum_south registered, goes to PE(i+1,j)

If I register the product:
- Cycle T: a_west and psum_north arrive (both registered)
- Combinational: product = a_west * w → prod_reg (registered at T+1)
- Cycle T+1: prod_reg available, but psum_north was for cycle T... 

The issue is that psum_north at cycle T corresponds to the sum from PE(i-1,j) for the activation that's currently at PE(i,j). If I delay the product by 1 cycle, the psum would need to be delayed by 1 cycle too. This means the psum path takes 2 cycles per PE instead of 1, adding N cycles total.

This won't preserve cycle accuracy. Let me abandon this approach.

One area optimization: **the deskew registers**. There are 28×32=896 bits of deskew registers. Plus the carry-save registers: 64×2×32=4096 bits. Plus weight: 64×8=512 bits. Plus activation: 64×8=512 bits. Plus skew: 28×8=224 bits. Plus valid: 15 bits. Total registers: ~6155 bits.

Actually, the sign extension of a signed 18-bit value to 32 bits is straightforward: `{{14{result[17]}}, result[17:0]}`. Let me try this.

input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [AW-1:0]\t  psum_north_s,\n    input [AW-1:0]\t  psum_north_c,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   // Sign-extend product to AW bits (product is 16 bits, AW=19)\n   wire [AW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(AW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n\n   wire [AW-1:0] cs_sum = psum_north_s ^ psum_north_c ^ prod_ext;\n   wire [AW-1:0] cs_carry = (psum_north_s & psum_north_c) |\n                              (psum_north_s & prod_ext) |\n                              (psum_north_c & prod_ext);\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_south_s <= {AW{1'b0}};\n         psum_south_c <= {AW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east     <= a_west;\n            psum_south_s <= cs_sum;\n            psum_south_c <= {cs_carry[AW-2:0], 1'b0};\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter AW = 19,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [AW-1:0] psum_s_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [AW-1:0] psum_c_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n [AW-1:0] result = psum_s_v[N][j] + psum_c_v[N][j];\n         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = {{(ACCW-AW){result[AW-1]}}, result};\n      end\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .AW(AW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .rst        (rst),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_north_s (psum_s_v[i][j]),\n               .psum_north_c (psum_c_v[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_south_s (psum_s_v[i+1][j]),\n               .psum_south_c (psum_c_v[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter AW = 19,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew\n         wire [DW-1:0] din =\n\t\t       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;\n         end\n         else begin : g_skewn\n            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= {DW{1'b0}};\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  sr[0] <= din;\n                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)\n #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .AW(AW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_array\n     (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .ld(ld), .en(en),\n      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), .c_bot(c_bot));\n\n   genvar j;\n   generate\n      for (j = 0; j Good improvement! Cost down to 2,893,914 (from 3,104,542). Area reduced from 4568 to 4339, delay from 679 to 667ps. The 19-bit truncation helps both area and delay.

Let me try to push further. Let me try 18-bit accumulation (checking if 2^17 = 131072 can be handled with carry-save sign extension):

Actually, the issue is that 8 * (-128) * (-128) = 131072 = 2^17. In signed 18-bit, the range is -2^17 to 2^17-1 = -131072 to 131071. So 131072 doesn't fit. But wait - let me double-check: can the value actually reach 131072? That requires all 8 products to be 16384 = (-128)*(-128). This happens when A[m][i] = -128 and B[i][j] = -128 for all i. Let me check the vectors to see if this case exists.

Actually, the spec says "directed zero/identity/extreme tiles, random tiles". The "extreme tiles" likely include -128 values. So 131072 is possible and 18 bits is not enough. Let me keep 19 bits.

But wait - in carry-save representation, the maximum representable range is different. With 19-bit sum and 19-bit carry (shifted left by 1), the maximum value is 2^19 - 1 + 2^19 - 2 = 2^20 - 3, which is much larger. The issue is whether the sign extension in carry-save works correctly for negative values.

In carry-save, I'm sign-extending the product to 19 bits: `{{(19-16){prod[15]}}, prod}`. This correctly sign-extends a 16-bit product to 19 bits. The CSA operates on these 19-bit values. The final propagate add `psum_s + psum_c` gives the correct 19-bit signed result, which is then sign-extended to 32 bits. This should be correct.

[ASSISTANT]
Higher target_delay (700) gives less area (4295) but more delay (722ps), so cost is worse. The default 500ps is best so far.

Now let me try a different approach. The critical path goes through the multiplier and the final propagate add. Let me try to **register the propagate add at the bottom** and compensate by using N-2-j deskew instead of N-1-j for all columns, while handling column N-1 specially.

Actually, let me try a different idea: what if the propagate add at the bottom is registered, but I reduce the input skew by 1 for all rows (except row 0)? Currently row i has skew=i. If I change to skew=i-1 for i>0 (and 0 for i=0), that reduces the total latency by 1 for rows 1-7. But row 0 has skew=0, so the total for row 0 becomes 0 + j + (N-1-0) + deskew = j + N-1 + deskew. For all rows to have the same latency, the skew needs to be exactly i. So I can't reduce it.

Let me try another approach: **register the propagate add and reduce deskew by 1 for ALL columns, including column N-1 which currently has deskew=0**. For column N-1, instead of 0 deskew with combinational add, I use 1 registered deskew with registered add. The total latency becomes: skew(i) + j + (N-1-i) + 1 = j + N + 1, which is 2N for the maximum. That's 1 more than 2N-1. So the valid pipeline would need to be LAT+1 = 16. But the testbench checks c_valid at exactly LAT=15 cycles. This won't work.

Hmm. Let me think about this differently. The propagate add adds delay but the critical path is the multiply (partial product tree) + CSA + propagate add. The propagate add is about 240ps out of 667ps total. If I could make the multiply faster, that would help more.

Let me try to **build the multiplier manually using a carry-save tree** so the multiply output is already in carry-save form, and then combine it with the psum using a 4:2 compressor. This would eliminate the propagate add within the multiplier (Yosys adds one) and the propagate add at the bottom.

A 4:2 compressor takes 4 inputs and produces 2 outputs (sum, carry). If I have (psum_s, psum_c, prod_s, prod_c), the 4:2 compressor gives (out_s, out_c). This would mean:
- The multiply produces partial products, and I sum them in a carry-save tree to get (prod_s, prod_c)
- The PE uses a 4:2 compressor: (psum_s, psum_c, prod_s, prod_c) → (out_s, out_c)
- Only at the bottom, a final propagate add is needed

The 4:2 compressor is just two levels of 3:2 CSAs, which is faster than a full add.

But building an 8×8 signed multiply with carry-save tree is complex. Let me try a simpler approach: use two 4:2 compressors to combine the 4 inputs. The multiply is still done by Yosys's `*`, but I split the product into two parts that can be added in carry-save.

Actually, here's a simpler idea: split the 16-bit product into high and low 8-bit halves. The low half (bits 0-7) goes into the CSA with the corresponding bits of psum. The high half (bits 8-15, sign-extended) goes into another CSA with the corresponding bits. But this is just the same as sign-extending and doing one CSA.

Let me try yet another approach: instead of a 3:2 CSA, use a **4:2 compressor** by splitting the product into sum and carry parts. The idea:
- `prod = a_west * w` (16 bits)
- Split prod into: `prod_s = prod & 0xAAAA` (even bits) and `prod_c = (prod & 0x5555) << 1` (odd bits shifted). Then `prod_s + prod_c = prod`. 
- Now use a 4:2 compressor: (psum_s, psum_c, prod_s, prod_c) → (out_s, out_c)

This way, the product doesn't need a propagate add, and the 4:2 compressor combines everything. Let me try:
  -> create_file(content="// Carry-save TPU with 4:2 compressor\n// Product split into even/odd bit carry-save, avoiding multiply propagate add\n// 4:2 compressor combines (psum_s, psum_c, prod_s, prod_c) -> (out_s, out_c)\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter AW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [AW-1:0]\t  psum_north_s,\n    input [AW-1:0]\t  psum_north_c,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [AW-1:0] psum_south_s,\n    output reg [AW-1:0] psum_south_c\n   );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   // Compute product (16 bits, signed)\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   \n   // Sign-extend to AW bits\n   wire [AW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(AW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n   \n   // Split product into carry-save: even bits -> sum, odd bits -> carry (shifted)\n   // prod = prod_even + prod_odd_shifted\n   // where prod_even = prod & 0x5555... and prod_odd = (prod & 0xAAAA...) >> 1, then shifted\n   wire [AW-1:0] prod_s = prod_ext & {{(AW-1){1'b0}}, 1'b1} | \n                          (prod_ext & {{(AW-3){1'b0}}, 2'b010, 1'b0} ? \n                           prod_ext & {{(AW-1){1'b0}}, 1'b1} : 0);\n   \n   // Actually, simpler: use prod directly as one input and 0 as carry\n   // Then it's just a 3:2 CSA. [ASSISTANT]
That was a mess with the 4:2 idea - it's actually the same as the 3:2 CSA. Let me abandon that direction and try a different optimization.

Let me think about what's actually on the critical path. From the timing report of design_cs19:
- Path: weight reg → multiply partial products (XOR/XNOR chain ~400ps) → CSA (MAJ ~70ps) → propagate add (~200ps) → deskew reg

The multiply is the dominant part. The propagate add is secondary.

Let me try a completely different approach to reduce the multiply delay: **use a Booth-encoded multiplier**. Booth encoding reduces the number of partial products from 8 to 4 (radix-4 Booth), which reduces the carry-save tree depth. But building Booth encoding manually is complex.

Alternatively, let me try to **pipeline the multiply** by registering the partial products. But that adds latency.

Actually, let me try a different strategy entirely. What if I **register the product in the PE** and compensate by restructuring the latency? 

Here's the key insight: if I register the product, the psum from the previous PE needs to be delayed by 1 cycle to match. But the psum arrives from the previous PE's registered output. If the previous PE also registers its product, the psum it produces is already delayed by 1 cycle relative to the activation. So the timing works out!

Let me think about this more carefully:

**Original PE (1 cycle):**
- Cycle T: a_west (from prev PE's a_east reg) and psum_north (from prev PE's psum_south reg) arrive
- Combinational: product = a_west * w; psum_south = psum_north + product
- Cycle T+1: psum_south and a_east are registered

**Product-registered PE (2 cycles):**
- Cycle T: a_west and psum_north arrive (both from registered outputs)
- Combinational: product = a_west * w → prod_reg (registered at T+1)
- Cycle T+1: prod_reg available, psum_north... wait, psum_north was valid at cycle T, not T+1.

The problem is that psum_north is only valid at cycle T (it changes at T+1 when the previous PE updates its psum_south). So at T+1, psum_north has already changed. I'd need to register psum_north too, adding another register.

**Two-register PE (2 cycles):**
- Cycle T: a_west and psum_north arrive
- Register: prod_reg <= a_west * w; psum_north_reg <= psum_north; a_east_reg <= a_west
- Cycle T+1: prod_reg, psum_north_reg available
- Combinational: psum_south = psum_north_reg + prod_reg
- Cycle T+2: psum_south registered

This adds 1 cycle per PE. With N=8 PEs in the psum chain, total extra = 8 cycles. Way too much.

But wait - what if only some PEs use the product-register approach? For example, only the bottom PE. The bottom PE is where the propagate add happens (critical path includes both multiply and propagate add). If I split the bottom PE's multiply and add into two cycles, I only add 1 cycle total, which I can compensate by reducing deskew by 1 for all columns.

But column N-1 has deskew=0. If I reduce by 1, it becomes -1. Unless I give column N-1 a deskew of 1 originally... but that would require changing the architecture.

Actually, what if I restructure so ALL columns have at least 1 deskew cycle? I can do this by increasing the input skew by 1 for all rows (including row 0). Currently row 0 has skew=0. If row 0 has skew=1, and I reduce all deskew by 1, the total stays the same. But column N-1 deskew would go from 0 to... I need to think about this differently.

Current: skew(i) = i, deskew(j) = N-1-j. Total = i + j + (N-1-i) + (N-1-j) = 2N-2. Plus registration = 2N-1.

If I add 1 to all skews: skew(i) = i+1, and use deskew(j) = N-1-j. Total = (i+1) + j + (N-1-i) + (N-1-j) = 2N-1. Plus registration = 2N. That's 1 too many.

If I add 1 to skews and subtract 1 from deskew: skew(i) = i+1, deskew(j) = N-2-j. Total = (i+1) + j + (N-1-i) + (N-2-j) = 2N-2. Same as before! But deskew for column N-1 = N-2-(N-1) = -1. Not possible.

What if I use the registered product only for the bottom row PE, and reduce deskew for that column by using a different deskew? The bottom-row PE is the one that produces the final carry-save result. If I register the propagate add, it adds 1 cycle for the bottom PE only. The total becomes 2N-1+1 = 2N for all columns. I need to reduce something by 1 to get back to 2N-1.

The propagate add is at the bottom, after the CSA chain. If I register it, the CSA chain still takes N-1 cycles, but the propagate add takes 1 more cycle. Total = skew(i) + j + (N-1-i) + 1 (propagate) + deskew(j). For this to be 2N-1: i + j + N-1-i + 1 + deskew = 2N-1, so deskew = N-1-j. Same deskew as before! The propagate add is just an extra stage.

Wait, but currently the propagate add is combinational (no extra cycle). If I register it, I add 1 cycle: total = skew(i) + j + (N-1-i) + 1 + deskew(j) = 2N-1 + 1 = 2N. I need deskew = N-2-j to get 2N-1. Column N-1 deskew = N-2-(N-1) = -1. Still impossible.

OK, this approach of registering the propagate add won't work with the current deskew scheme. Let me try a different idea.

**New idea: Make the bottom propagate add part of the deskew chain for columns that have deskew > 0.** For column N-1 (deskew=0), the propagate add stays combinational. For all other columns, the propagate add is registered as the first deskew stage.

This doesn't help column N-1 which still has the combinational propagate add. But it might help the overall area or delay if column N-1 is not the critical path.

Actually, let me look at the timing path again. The critical path is through column 4 (PE row 6, col 4). The propagate add is at the bottom of column 4, which has deskew = N-1-4 = 3. So the propagate add feeds into a 3-stage deskew. The critical path includes the propagate add as a combinational step before the first deskew register.

If I register the propagate add for columns with deskew > 0 (columns 0-6), I add 1 cycle to those columns. The total for column j (j < N-1) becomes: 2N-1 + 1 - deskew_reduction. If I reduce deskew by 1 for columns 0-6, total stays 2N-1. For column N-1, the propagate add stays combinational (deskew=0, total=2N-1).

So: 
- Columns 0-6: deskew = N-2-j (reduced by 1), with registered propagate add as the first deskew stage
- Column 7: deskew = 0, with combinational propagate add

This preserves cycle accuracy! And the critical path for columns 0-6 becomes: CSA_reg → propagate_add → register (splitting the path). The propagate add now only needs to reach the first register, not go through the deskew chain.

For column 7: CSA_reg → propagate_add → c_data (still combinational, but column 7 might not be the critical path).

Let me implement this:

Ah, I see the problem! The carry values from the bottom of the array are in AW=19 bits, but I'm sign-extending to 32 bits. The carry in carry-save is shifted left by 1, so its LSB is always 0. The sign extension of the carry should be correct, but let me check...

Actually, the issue might be that `psum_c_v[N][j]` has its MSB as a sign bit only if the carry propagation hasn't overflowed. In carry-save, the carry vector is NOT a signed number - it's the carry part of a carry-save representation. Sign-extending it is wrong!

The correct approach: the carry-save pair (s, c) represents the value `s + c`. To extend to wider bits, I need to sign-extend the *sum* `s + c`, not each part independently. But I can't do that without the propagate add.

So the sign extension of carry-save to 32 bits is incorrect. The fix: do the propagate add in AW bits, then sign-extend. That's what design_cs19 does (the propagate add is `psum_s_v[N][j] + psum_c_v[N][j]` in AW bits, then sign-extend).

In my new design, I'm passing `c_bot_s` and `c_bot_c` as 32-bit sign-extended values, which is wrong for the carry. Let me fix this by passing the AW-bit values and doing the propagate add at the deskew:

- Row i of A enters the array at cycle i (input skew)

- PE(i,j) registers the psum, so the product is computed at cycle i+j and the psum appears at cycle i+j+1

Yosys synthesizes this as an array of partial products followed by a carry-save tree and a final propagate add. The propagate add within the multiply is part of the critical path.

What if I use a smaller multiplier? Since the weight is 8-bit and activation is 8-bit, the product is 16-bit. But in carry-save, I could use a 4-bit × 8-bit multiply (splitting the activation into two 4-bit halves), producing two 12-bit partial products that are already shifted. Then the CSA combines them with the psum. But earlier this had sign issues.

Let me think about the sign handling for the split multiply. For a signed 8-bit number a = {a7, a6:0}, we have:
- a = sign_extend(a7) * (-2^7) + {a6:0} (for unsigned lower 7 bits)
- Actually, a = -a7*2^7 + a6:0 (where a6:0 is unsigned 0-127)

So a*w = (-a7*2^7 + a6:0) * w = -a7*w*2^7 + a6:0*w

If I compute a6:0*w (7-bit × 8-bit unsigned = 15-bit) and conditionally subtract w<<7, that's:
- prod = a6_0 * w - (a7 ? w << 7 : 0)

But this is still a multiply plus a conditional subtract. The conditional subtract might not be faster.

Let me try another approach: **Baugh-Wooley multiplier** for signed multiplication. This modifies the partial products to handle signs correctly without needing a final subtraction. The Baugh-Wooley method flips certain partial products and adds correction constants, resulting in all-positive additions.

But this is getting complex. Let me try a different angle: instead of optimizing the PE, let me try to **reduce area** by sharing resources or reducing register count.

One area idea: the deskew chain has 28×32 = 896 bits of 32-bit registers. If I deskew in the carry-save domain (AW=19 bits, two vectors), I need 28×2×19 = 1064 bits. That's more! So carry-save deskew is worse for area.

But if I keep the propagate add at the bottom (combinational) and deskew in 32-bit, the area is 28×32 = 896 bits. The propagate add adds combinational logic. This is what design_cs19 does.

Let me try to reduce the deskew area by deskewing only the needed bits (19 bits) and sign-extending at the output:
  -> read_file(filename='design_cs19.sv', offset=155, limit=30)

      end
   endgenerate

     (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .ld(ld), .en(en),
      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), .c_bot(c_bot));

   generate
      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_deskew
         localparam D = N - 1 - j;
         wire [ACCW-1:0] cin = c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW];
         if (D == 0) begin : g_deskew0
            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = cin;
         end

            reg [ACCW-1:0] dr [0:D-1];
            integer k;

               if (rst) begin
                  for (k = 0; k < D; k = k + 1)
                    dr[k] <= {ACCW{1'b0}};
               end

                  dr[0] <= cin;
                  for (k = 1; k < D; k = k + 1)

[ASSISTANT]
This is about the same as design_cs_reg (2884780 vs 2881138). The propagate add is still on the critical path.

Let me try a more radical approach. The critical path is: weight_reg → multiply (partial products + CSA tree, ~420ps) → CSA (with psum, ~70ps) → propagate add (~180ps) → deskew_reg.

The multiply and propagate add are both significant. Let me try to **eliminate the propagate add entirely** by keeping everything in carry-save until the final output, and doing the propagate add only at the output (combinational, but for column N-1 which has deskew=0, the path is: CSA_reg → propagate_add → output).

But I need to check: for column N-1, the path would be CSA_reg → propagate_add → c_data. The propagate add is a 19-bit add (~180ps). The CSA_reg → propagate_add path is only 180ps, which is much less than 664ps. So column N-1 would NOT be the critical path anymore!

The critical path would shift to the multiply + CSA in the PEs (which is ~490ps), plus the deskew for column 0 (which would carry carry-save through 7 stages → propagate_add → output). For column 0: CSA_reg → deskew_reg → ... → deskew_reg → propagate_add → output. The propagate add is at the end, after the deskew chain. The critical path for column 0 is: CSA_reg → first deskew_reg (carry-save), then subsequent deskew stages, then propagate_add → output. But the last stage is: deskew_reg → propagate_add → c_data (combinational). The propagate add is ~180ps, much less than the multiply+CSA path.

So the critical path would be: multiply + CSA → register (in PE), which is ~490ps. That's a significant improvement from 664ps!

But I need carry-save deskew registers (2×19 = 38 bits per stage) instead of 32-bit deskew registers. Let me check the area tradeoff: 28×38 = 1064 vs 28×32 = 896. That's 168 more register bits. But the area might still be better overall due to the simpler combinational logic.

Let me implement this:

Let me try to optimize the multiplier. The 8×8 signed multiply is the biggest part of the critical path (~420ps). Let me try using a **Baugh-Wooley multiplier** which handles signed multiplication with all-positive partial products.

Actually, let me try a simpler approach: manually implement the multiply using unsigned multiply + sign correction. For signed a × w:
- If a and w have the same sign: result = |a| × |w| (positive)
- If different signs: result = -(|a| × |w|)

This is: `(|a| * |w|) * sign(a == sign(w) ? +1 : -1)`

But computing |a| and |w| requires a 2's complement operation, and the conditional negation adds logic. This might not be faster.

Let me try another approach: **Baugh-Wooley method**. For a × b (both N-bit signed):
- Partial product p_ij = a_i AND b_j for i < N-1, j < N-1
- For the sign bits: complement certain partial products and add correction constants

The Baugh-Wooley multiplier converts the signed multiplication into unsigned-like partial products by complementing the sign-bit partial products. This avoids the need for a separate sign correction step.

But implementing this manually is complex and Yosys might already do something similar. Let me try a different approach.

Let me try to **reduce the number of partial products** using radix-4 Booth encoding. This halves the number of partial products from 8 to 4, reducing the carry-save tree depth by 1 level.

Let me try implementing Booth radix-4 encoding manually:

For Booth radix-4, we encode the 8-bit operand `a` into 4 Booth digits, each selecting from {0, w, -w, 2w, -2w}.

Let me try this:
  -> create_file(content="// Carry-save TPU with Booth radix-4 encoded multiplier\n// Reduces partial products from 8 to 4, reducing multiply delay\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter AW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [AW-1:0]\t  psum_north_s,\n    input [AW-1:0]\t  psum_north_c,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [AW-1:0] psum_south_s,\n    output reg [AW-1:0] psum_south_c\n   );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   // Booth radix-4 encoding of a_west\n   // Extend a_west with a leading 0 for Booth encoding\n   wire [DW:0] a_ext = {1'b0, a_west};\n   \n   // 4 Booth digits for 8-bit operand\n   // Each group of 3 bits: a[2i+1], a[2i], a[2i-1] (with a[-1]=0)\n   wire [2:0] b0 = {a_ext[1], a_ext[0], 1'b0};      // bits 1,0,-1\n   wire [2:0] b1 = {a_ext[3], a_ext[2], a_ext[1]};   // bits 3,2,1\n   wire [2:0] b2 = {a_ext[5], a_ext[4], a_ext[3]};   // bits 5,4,3\n   wire [2:0] b3 = {a_ext[7], a_ext[6], a_ext[5]};   // bits 7,6,5\n   \n   // Booth decode: select 0, +w, -w, +2w, -2w\n   // 000: 0, 001: +w, 010: +w, 011: +2w, 100: -2w, 101: -w, 110: -w, 111: 0\n   \n   // Partial product for Booth digit b, shifted by 2*idx\n   // Each partial product is (DW+2) bits wide to handle -2w\n   function [DW+1:0] booth_pp;\n      input [2:0] b;\n      input [DW-1:0] weight;\n      reg [DW:0] w_neg;\n      begin\n         w_neg = ~{w, 1'b0} + 1;  // -2w in (DW+1) bits\n         case (b)\n            3'b000: booth_pp = {(DW+2){1'b0}};\n            3'b001: booth_pp = {{2{w[DW-1]}}, w};        // +w\n            3'b010: booth_pp = {{2{w[DW-1]}}, w};        // +w\n            3'b011: booth_pp = {1'b0, w, 1'b0};          // +2w (no sign ext needed since shifted)\n            3'b100: booth_pp = {1'b1, w_neg};            // -2w\n            3'b101: booth_pp = {{2{~w[DW-1]}}, ~w + 1};  // -w\n            3'b110: booth_pp = {{2{~w[DW-1]}}, ~w + 1};  // -w\n            3'b111: booth_pp = {(DW+2){1'b0}};\n         endcase\n      end\n   endfunction\n   \n   // This is getting too complex and error-prone\n   // Let me just use the standard multiply\n   \n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   wire [AW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(AW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n\n   wire [AW-1:0] cs_sum = psum_north_s ^ psum_north_c ^ prod_ext;\n   wire [AW-1:0] cs_carry = (psum_north_s & psum_north_c) |\n                              (psum_north_s & prod_ext) |\n                              (psum_north_c & prod_ext);\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_south_s <= {AW{1'b0}};\n         psum_south_c <= {AW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east     <= a_west;\n            psum_south_s <= cs_sum;\n            psum_south_c <= {cs_carry[AW-2:0], 1'b0};\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter AW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*AW-1:0]\tc_bot_s,\n    output [N*AW-1:0]\tc_bot_c\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [AW-1:0] psum_s_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [AW-1:0] psum_c_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0;
