  -> create_file(content="// Carry-save TPU with 19-bit deskew (registered propagate add, 19-bit deskew, sign-extend at output)\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter AW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [AW-1:0]\t  psum_north_s,\n    input [AW-1:0]\t  psum_north_c,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [AW-1:0] psum_south_s,\n    output reg [AW-1:0] psum_south_c\n   );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   wire [AW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(AW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n\n   wire [AW-1:0] cs_sum = psum_north_s ^ psum_north_c ^ prod_ext;\n   wire [AW-1:0] cs_carry = (psum_north_s & psum_north_c) |\n                              (psum_north_s & prod_ext) |\n                              (psum_north_c & prod_ext);\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_south_s <= {AW{1'b0}};\n         psum_south_c <= {AW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east     <= a_west;\n            psum_south_s <= cs_sum;\n            psum_south_c <= {cs_carry[AW-2:0], 1'b0};\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter AW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*AW-1:0]\tc_bot_s,\n    output [N*AW-1:0]\tc_bot_c\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [AW-1:0] psum_s_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [AW-1:0] psum_c_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_s_v[0][j] = {AW{1'b0}};\n         assign psum_c_v[0][j] = {AW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         assign c_bot_s[j*AW +: AW] = psum_s_v[N][j];\n         assign c_bot_c[j*AW +: AW] = psum_c_v[N][j];\n      end\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .AW(AW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .rst        (rst),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_north_s (psum_s_v[i][j]),\n               .psum_north_c (psum_c_v[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_south_s (psum_s_v[i+1][j]),\n               .psum_south_c (psum_c_v[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter AW = 19,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew\n         wire [DW-1:0] din =\n\t\t       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;\n         end\n         else begin : g_skewn\n            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= {DW{1'b0}};\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  sr[0] <= din;\n                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= sr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = sr[i-1];\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   wire [N*AW-1:0] c_bot_s, c_bot_c;\n   tpu_array #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .AW(AW)) u_array\n     (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .ld(ld), .en(en),\n      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), \n      .c_bot_s(c_bot_s), .c_bot_c(c_bot_c));\n\n   // Deskew: registered propagate add (AW bits) as stage 0,\n   // then (D-1) AW-bit deskew stages, sign-extend at output\n   genvar j;\n   generate\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_deskew\n         localparam D = N - 1 - j;\n         wire [AW-1:0] cs_s = c_bot_s[j*AW +: AW];\n         wire [AW-1:0] cs_c = c_bot_c[j*AW +: AW];\n         wire [AW-1:0] prop = cs_s + cs_c;\n         \n         if (D == 0) begin : g_deskew0\n            // Column N-1: combinational propagate add, sign-extend\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = {{(ACCW-AW){prop[AW-1]}}, prop};\n         end\n         else begin : g_deskewn\n            // D stages of AW-bit registers\n            // Stage 0: registered propagate add\n            // Stages 1..D-1: deskew\n            reg [AW-1:0] dr [0:D-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; k < D; k = k + 1)\n                    dr[k] <= {AW{1'b0}};\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  dr[0] <= prop;\n                  for (k = 1; k < D; k = k + 1)\n                    dr[k] <= dr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            // Sign-extend at output\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = {{(ACCW-AW){dr[D-1][AW-1]}}, dr[D-1]};\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   reg [LAT-1:0] vpipe;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst)\n        vpipe <= {LAT{1'b0}};\n      else if (en)\n        vpipe <= {vpipe[LAT-2:0], a_valid};\n   end\n   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];\n\nendmodule\n", filename='design_cs19c.sv')

[ASSISTANT]

Now let me think about the key bottleneck. The critical path is: weight_reg → multiply partial products (~420ps) → CSA (~70ps) → propagate add (~180ps) → deskew_reg. The multiply is the dominant part.

Let me try to implement a proper Booth radix-4 multiplier to reduce the partial product count from 8 to 4. This should reduce the multiply delay significantly.

For an 8-bit signed number `a` and 8-bit signed number `w`, Booth radix-4 encoding:
- Pad `a` to 9 bits: `{a[7], a[7:0]}` (sign extend) or `{1'b0, a[7:0]}` (zero extend for Booth)
- Actually, for modified Booth encoding, we pad with a 0 at the LSB: `a_booth = {a[7:0], 1'b0}`
- Then group into 4 overlapping triples: {a[1:0],0}, {a[3:2],a[1]}, {a[5:4],a[3]}, {a[7:6],a[5]}
- Each triple selects: 000→0, 001→+w, 010→+w, 011→+2w, 100→-2w, 101→-w, 110→-w, 111→0
- The partial products are shifted by 0, 2, 4, 6 bits

For signed multiplication, the last Booth digit needs sign extension handling.

Let me implement this properly:
  -> create_file(content="// Carry-save TPU with Booth radix-4 multiplier\n// 4 partial products instead of 8, reducing multiply critical path\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter AW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [AW-1:0]\t  psum_north_s,\n    input [AW-1:0]\t  psum_north_c,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [AW-1:0] psum_south_s,\n    output reg [AW-1:0] psum_south_c\n   );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   // Booth radix-4 encoding of a_west\n   // Pad with 0 at LSB: a_pad = {a_west, 1'b0}\n   // Groups of 3 overlapping bits:\n   wire [2:0] g0 = {a_west[1:0], 1'b0};\n   wire [2:0] g1 = {a_west[3:2], a_west[1]};\n   wire [2:0] g2 = {a_west[5:4], a_west[3]};\n   wire [2:0] g3 = {a_west[7:6], a_west[5]};\n\n   // Booth decode: select from {0, +w, -w, +2w, -2w}\n   // 000: 0, 001/010: +w, 011: +2w, 100: -2w, 101/110: -w, 111: 0\n   \n   // Partial product is (DW+2) bits wide (to accommodate 2w and sign)\n   // For groups 0,1,2: partial product is sign-extended\n   // For group 3 (MSB): needs special sign handling for signed multiply\n   \n   // neg_w = -w (two's complement, DW+1 bits)\n   wire [DW:0] neg_w = ~{1'b0, w} + 1;\n   // neg_2w = -2w (DW+2 bits)\n   wire [DW+1:0] neg_2w = ~{1'b0, w, 1'b0} + 1;\n   // pos_2w = 2w\n   wire [DW+1:0] pos_2w = {1'b0, w, 1'b0};\n   // pos_w = w (sign-extended to DW+2 bits)\n   wire [DW+1:0] pos_w = {{2{w[DW-1]}}, w};\n   // neg_w_ext = -w (sign-extended to DW+2 bits)\n   wire [DW+1:0] neg_w_ext = ~{{2{w[DW-1]}}, w} + 1;\n   \n   // Select partial product for each group\n   reg [DW+1:0] pp0, pp1, pp2, pp3;\n   always @(*) begin\n      case (g0)\n         3'b000: pp0 = {(DW+2){1'b0}};\n         3'b001, 3'b010: pp0 = pos_w;\n         3'b011: pp0 = pos_2w;\n         3'b100: pp0 = neg_2w;\n         3'b101, 3'b110: pp0 = neg_w_ext;\n         3'b111: pp0 = {(DW+2){1'b0}};\n      endcase\n      case (g1)\n         3'b000: pp1 = {(DW+2){1'b0}};\n         3'b001, 3'b010: pp1 = pos_w;\n         3'b011: pp1 = pos_2w;\n         3'b100: pp1 = neg_2w;\n         3'b101, 3'b110: pp1 = neg_w_ext;\n         3'b111: pp1 = {(DW+2){1'b0}};\n      endcase\n      case (g2)\n         3'b000: pp2 = {(DW+2){1'b0}};\n         3'b001, 3'b010: pp2 = pos_w;\n         3'b011: pp2 = pos_2w;\n         3'b100: pp2 = neg_2w;\n         3'b101, 3'b110: pp2 = neg_w_ext;\n         3'b111: pp2 = {(DW+2){1'b0}};\n      endcase\n      case (g3)\n         3'b000: pp3 = {(DW+2){1'b0}};\n         3'b001, 3'b010: pp3 = pos_w;\n         3'b011: pp3 = pos_2w;\n         3'b100: pp3 = neg_2w;\n         3'b101, 3'b110: pp3 = neg_w_ext;\n         3'b111: pp3 = {(DW+2){1'b0}};\n      endcase\n   end\n\n   // Sign-extend partial products and shift\n   // pp0 at bit 0, pp1 at bit 2, pp2 at bit 4, pp3 at bit 6\n   // For signed multiply, the MSB group (pp3) sign needs extension\n   // Total product is 2*DW = 16 bits\n   \n   wire [AW-1:0] pp0_ext = {{(AW-DW-2){pp0[DW+1]}}, pp0};\n   wire [AW-1:0] pp1_ext = {{(AW-DW-2){pp1[DW+1]}}, pp1} << 2;\n   wire [AW-1:0] pp2_ext = {{(AW-DW-2){pp2[DW+1]}}, pp2} << 4;\n   wire [AW-1:0] pp3_ext = {{(AW-DW-2){pp3[DW+1]}}, pp3} << 6;\n\n   // Sum partial products (Yosys will optimize this)\n   wire [AW-1:0] prod_ext = pp0_ext + pp1_ext + pp2_ext + pp3_ext;\n\n   // 3:2 CSA with psum\n   wire [AW-1:0] cs_sum = psum_north_s ^ psum_north_c ^ prod_ext;\n   wire [AW-1:0] cs_carry = (psum_north_s & psum_north_c) |\n                              (psum_north_s & prod_ext) |\n                              (psum_north_c & prod_ext);\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_south_s <= {AW{1'b0}};\n         psum_south_c <= {AW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east     <= a_west;\n            psum_south_s <= cs_sum;\n            psum_south_c <= {cs_carry[AW-2:0], 1'b0};\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter AW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*AW-1:0]\tc_bot_s,\n    output [N*AW-1:0]\tc_bot_c\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h [ASSISTANT]
The Booth multiplier has bugs. The expected values show sign issues - the values are off. The Booth encoding for signed multiplication needs careful sign extension handling. 

Looking at the error: `exp=0002000000020000... got=fffe0000fffe0000...`. Expected +2 but got -2. This suggests the sign of the product is flipped. The issue is likely in how I handle the MSB Booth group for signed numbers.

The problem with my Booth implementation: for signed multiplication, the MSB group needs special treatment. In standard modified Booth encoding for signed numbers, the sign bit of the operand needs to be properly handled. My current implementation pads with `{a_west, 1'b0}` which doesn't handle the sign correctly for the MSB group.

Let me abandon the Booth approach (it's error-prone) and go back to the best design. Let me instead try to reduce area by other means. Let me try using `neg_w_ext = ~{{2{w[DW-1]}}, w} + 1` which involves an adder - this is expensive. 

Let me go back to design_cs_reg.sv (the best) and try a different optimization: **remove the a_east register by reusing the activation skew**. The activation flows west-to-east through the PEs, and each PE registers `a_east`. But the activation also passes through the input skew chain. Can I eliminate some registers?

Actually, the `a_east` register in each PE is necessary for the systolic pipeline - it delays the activation by 1 cycle per column. Without it, the activation would arrive at all PEs in a row simultaneously, breaking the column timing.

Let me try another area optimization: the weight register `w` needs a mux for load vs hold. Currently, `if (ld) w <= w_north;`. This creates a mux. But during compute (ld=0), w doesn't change. Yosys should optimize this to just a CE (clock enable) on the register. This should already be optimal.

Let me try a different approach: **reduce the number of deskew stages by changing the skew/deskew split**. Currently skew(i) = i and deskew(j) = N-1-j. What if I use skew(i) = N-1 and deskew(j) = j? Then the total is the same: (N-1) + j + (N-1-i) + j = 2N-2-i+2j... no, that doesn't work.

Actually, the skew must be i for the psum to work correctly (each row enters at a different time so the partial sums chain correctly). The deskew must be N-1-j for the outputs to align. These are fundamental to the design.

Let me try to optimize the deskew by using a single shared shift register per column instead of separate registers. Actually, that's what the current design does - it's a chain of registers.

Let me try reducing the deskew width. In design_cs_reg, the deskew uses 32-bit registers. If I deskew in 19 bits (AW) and sign-extend at output, I save area. But I tried this in design_cs19c and it was worse (delay went up). The issue might be that the sign extension at the output adds combinational delay.

Wait, let me look at design_cs_reg more carefully. In that design, the propagate add is done in AW=19 bits, sign-extended to 32 bits, and then deskewed in 32 bits. What if I deskew in 19 bits and sign-extend at the very end?

Good, reverted to the best: cost 2,881,138, delay 664ps, area 4338.

Now let me analyze the critical path more carefully. The path goes:
1. Weight register Q (99.64ps)
2. NOR3 → partial product generation (~108ps) - this is multiply partial products
3. XOR/XNOR chain (~240ps) - this is the multiply carry-save tree
4. MAJ/CSA gates (~70ps) - this is the 3:2 CSA with psum
5. O2A1O1I/OAI gates (~180ps) - this is the propagate add at the bottom

So the multiply (partial products + carry-save tree) takes ~350ps, CSA ~70ps, propagate add ~180ps. Total ~660ps.

The propagate add at the bottom adds ~180ps. If I could eliminate it, the path would be ~420ps. But I've already tried carry-save deskew and it didn't help (area went up).

Let me try a different approach to reduce the propagate add delay: use a **carry-lookahead adder** or **prefix adder** for the 19-bit propagate add. Yosys might be using a ripple-carry adder which is slow.

Actually, let me try something creative: what if the propagate add is done in **two stages** - first the lower bits (fast, no carry dependency) and then the upper bits? Or what if I use a carry-select approach?

Actually, Yosys should already be using an optimized adder. The 19-bit add is about 180ps, which is reasonable. The main bottleneck is the multiply.

Let me try a completely different approach: **reduce the multiply operand width**. Since the product is sign-extended to 19 bits, and 8×8=16 bits, the product fits in 16 bits. But the multiply itself generates 8 partial products. What if I split the 8-bit multiply into two 4-bit multiplies?

For a signed 8-bit number a = {a_sign, a_low[6:0]} where a = -a_sign*128 + a_low:
- a * w = (-a_sign*128 + a_low) * w = -a_sign * w * 128 + a_low * w
- a_low * w is a 7×8 unsigned multiply = 15 bits
- w * 128 is just w shifted left by 7

So: prod = (a_low * w) - (a_sign ? w << 7 : 0)

The 7×8 unsigned multiply has 7 partial products instead of 8 (minor improvement). But the conditional subtract adds a 16-bit subtract.

Actually, let me try a different decomposition. Split a into high and low 4-bit halves:
- a = a_hi * 16 + a_lo (where a_hi = a[7:4], a_lo = a[3:0])
- But for signed: a = (a_hi_signed * 16) + a_lo, where a_hi_signed sign-extends

This doesn't simplify things. Let me try yet another approach.

**What if I use a LUT-based multiplier?** Since the weight is stationary, I could precompute a lookup table during weight load. For each possible 8-bit activation value (256 values), store the product w * activation. This would replace the 8×8 multiply with a 256-entry × 16-bit LUT, which is just a register array with a mux. The critical path would be: activation_reg → LUT_read → CSA, which might be faster.

But 256 × 16 = 4096 bits per PE, × 64 PEs = 262,144 bits. That's way too much area!

What about a smaller LUT? Split the activation into two 4-bit halves: 16 × 16 = 256 bits per half, × 2 = 512 bits per PE. Each half-LUT gives a 12-bit product (4-bit × 8-bit). The critical path would be: activation_reg → LUT_read → shift+add → CSA. The LUT read is fast (just a mux), but the shift+add adds back the multiply complexity.

Actually, the LUT approach with 4-bit halves would give:
- LUT_hi[a_hi[3:0]] = a_hi * w (12-bit signed, but a_hi is unsigned 0-15, w is 8-bit signed)
- LUT_lo[a_lo[3:0]] = a_lo * w (12-bit unsigned)
- prod = (LUT_hi << 4) + LUT_lo

Each LUT is 16 entries × 12 bits = 192 bits per PE. The critical path is: a_west_reg → LUT lookup (mux) → shift + add → CSA. The LUT lookup is a 16:1 mux, which is fast. The shift+add is a 12-bit add.

But wait, for signed a: a = {a[7], a[6:0]}. If a[7]=1 (negative), the high nibble a_hi = a[7:4] represents a value from 8 to 15 in unsigned, but signed it's -8 to -1. So I need to handle the sign of the high nibble.

Actually, for a two's complement number a = a_hi*16 + a_lo where a_hi and a_lo are unsigned nibbles:
- a = a_hi*16 + a_lo
- a * w = a_hi*w*16 + a_lo*w

But a_hi is unsigned (0-15), w is signed (-128 to 127). So a_hi*w is a 4-bit×8-bit signed multiply, result is 12-bit signed. And a_lo*w is 4-bit×8-bit unsigned×signed, result is 12-bit. The final product is (a_hi*w << 4) + a_lo*w, sign-extended.

This approach uses two 16-entry LUTs per PE. Let me try it:

Actually, this is getting complex. Let me try a simpler optimization first: **use the $signed product but with explicit partial product decomposition that might help Yosys**.

Let me try computing the product as `a * w = (a + 128) * w - 128 * w`. The idea is to shift a to unsigned: `a + 128` is unsigned (0-255). Then `(a+128)*w` is an 8-bit×8-bit mixed multiply, and `128*w` is a constant (during compute, w is fixed). But this doesn't help with the multiply itself.

Let me try something different: **let Yosys handle the multiply but with a higher target_delay to let it use a smaller multiplier**. Wait, I already tried that and it increased delay.

Let me try a radical approach: **eliminate the propagate add by absorbing it into the last PE's CSA**. In the last PE (bottom row), instead of outputting carry-save (s, c), do the propagate add internally and output the result. But the propagate add is still on the critical path.

Actually, what if I register the propagate add in the bottom PE and reduce the deskew for all columns by 1? The issue is column N-1 has deskew=0. But what if I add 1 extra input skew cycle for row 0 only, and reduce deskew by 1 for all columns?

Current: skew(0)=0, skew(i)=i for i>0. deskew(j) = N-1-j.
If I change to: skew(0)=1, skew(i)=i+1 for i>0. deskew(j) = N-2-j.
Then total for any (i,j): (i+1) + j + (N-1-i) + (N-2-j) = 2N-2. Same!
But deskew(N-1) = N-2-(N-1) = -1. Still impossible!

The fundamental issue is that column N-1 has 0 deskew because it's the last column, and the activate reaches it last. Adding 1 to all skews adds 1 to the total, which I'd need to subtract from deskew, but column N-1 can't go below 0.

Wait, what if I add 1 to all skews AND add 1 to all deskews? Total = (i+1) + j + (N-1-i) + (N-1-j+1) = 2N. That's 2N, which is 1 more than 2N-1.

What if the PE pipeline adds 1 cycle? Currently the PE takes 1 cycle (multiply+CSA → register). If the bottom PE also registers the propagate add, it takes 2 cycles for the bottom PE. But all other PEs take 1 cycle. The total for column j: skew(i) + j + (N-1-i-1) + 2 (bottom PE, 2 cycles) + deskew(j) = i + j + N-2-i + 2 + deskew = j + N + deskew. For this to be 2N-1: deskew = N-1-j. Same deskew! And the total is j + N + N-1-j = 2N-1. ✓

So if only the bottom PE takes 2 cycles (registered propagate add), and all other PEs take 1 cycle, the latency is preserved! But wait, the bottom PE's psum path is: CSA → register → propagate_add → register. That's 2 cycles for the bottom PE. The other PEs take 1 cycle. The psum from PE(N-2,j) arrives at PE(N-1,j) at cycle T. The CSA is computed and registered at T+1. The propagate add is computed and registered at T+2. So the bottom PE outputs at T+2 instead of T+1. The total for column j: skew(i) + j + (N-2-i) (for PEs 0..N-2) + 2 (bottom PE) + deskew(j) = i + j + N-2-i + 2 + deskew = j + N + deskew. For 2N-1: deskew = N-1-j. Same!

This works! The bottom PE does CSA → reg → propagate_add → reg (2 cycles), and all other PEs do multiply+CSA → reg (1 cycle). The deskew is the same (N-1-j). The total latency is 2N-1. ✓

The critical path for the bottom PE: CSA_reg → propagate_add → reg. This is just a 19-bit add (~180ps), much less than the current 664ps!

The critical path for other PEs: multiply → CSA → reg. This is ~490ps (multiply ~420 + CSA ~70).

So the overall critical path becomes ~490ps (from non-bottom PEs), a significant improvement from 664ps!

Wait, the new total already includes the registration. Let me be more careful.

In the original design:
- A row enters at cycle 0 (a_valid asserted)
- Row i of A enters the array at cycle i (after skew)
- Activation reaches PE(i,j) at cycle i+j
- PE(i,j) computes and registers psum: available at cycle i+j+1
- Psum propagates south: PE(i+1,j) at i+j+1, PE(i+2,j) at i+j+2, ..., PE(N-1,j) at i+j+(N-1-i)
- PE(N-1,j) output (psum_south): registered at cycle i+j+(N-1-i)+1 = j+N
- Combinational propagate add: available at cycle j+N (same cycle, combinational)
- Deskew: N-1-j stages, each 1 cycle. Available at cycle j+N+(N-1-j) = 2N-1

So the original total is 2N-1.

In the new design:
- Row i of A enters at cycle i
- Activation reaches PE(i,j) at cycle i+j
- For i < N-1: PE(i,j) registers psum at i+j+1. Psum propagates to PE(i+1,j) at i+j+1.
- For i = N-1: PE(N-1,j) receives psum at cycle N-1+j. CSA registered at N-1+j+1 = N+j. Propagate add registered at N+j+1.
- Deskew: N-1-j stages. Available at N+j+1 + (N-1-j) = 2N.

That's 2N = 16, not 2N-1 = 15. One too many!

The issue is that the bottom PE takes 2 cycles (CSA reg + propagate add reg), while the original took 1 cycle (CSA reg) + 0 (combinational propagate add). So I'm adding 1 extra cycle. I need to compensate by reducing deskew by 1. But column N-1 has deskew=0.

So the fundamental problem remains: column N-1 can't have its deskew reduced below 0.

Unless... I change the skew/deskew split. What if I increase the input skew by 1 for all rows (including row 0)?

New skew: skew(0)=1, skew(i)=i+1. New deskew: deskew(j)=N-1-j (same).

Total: (i+1) + j + (N-1-i) + (N-1-j) = 2N-1 (with registration). This is 1 more than before (2N-2+1=2N-1... wait, that's the same as original).

Hmm, let me think differently. The original total with registration is 2N-1. If I add 1 cycle to the bottom PE and 1 to the input skew, the total becomes 2N-1+1 = 2N. I need to subtract 1 from deskew, but column N-1 can't go below 0.

The only way to make this work is to NOT add 1 to the input skew, and instead absorb the extra cycle by reducing deskew for columns 0..N-2 (which have deskew ≥ 1) and keeping column N-1 with the extra cycle.

For column j (j < N-1): total = i + j + (N-i) + (N-2-j) = 2N-2. With registration = 2N-1. ✓ (deskew reduced by 1)
For column N-1: total = i + (N-1) + (N-i) + 0 = 2N-1. With registration = 2N. ✗ (1 too many)

Column N-1 is still 1 too many. The bottom PE for column N-1 takes 2 cycles, and there's no deskew to compensate.

What if the bottom PE for column N-1 does the propagate add combinationally (not registered)? Then it takes 1 cycle (same as original), and the propagate add is on the critical path for that column only. For other columns, the propagate add is registered (2 cycles for bottom PE), and deskew is reduced by 1.

For column j (j < N-1): bottom PE takes 2 cycles, deskew = N-2-j. Total = i + j + (N-i) + (N-2-j) = 2N-2. With reg = 2N-1. ✓
For column N-1: bottom PE takes 1 cycle (combinational propagate add), deskew = 0. Total = i + (N-1) + (N-1-i) + 0 = 2N-2. With reg = 2N-1. ✓

This works! Column N-1 keeps the combinational propagate add (same as before), while columns 0..N-2 have the registered propagate add with reduced deskew.

But this is exactly what design_cs_reg already does! The propagate add is registered for columns 0..N-2 (as the first deskew stage) and combinational for column N-1.

So design_cs_reg is already the optimal approach for this strategy. The critical path is still through the multiply + CSA + propagate add (for column N-1, which is combinational).

Wait, but the critical path in design_cs_reg goes through column 1 (PE row 4, col 1), not column N-1. Let me re-examine...

The critical path endpoint is `vh_b2dfceb04e2f` which is a deskew register. For column 1, the deskew is N-1-1 = 6 stages. The first stage is the registered propagate add. The path from the PE to this register goes through: multiply + CSA + propagate add. The propagate add is combinational before the register.

So the critical path for columns 0..N-2 is: multiply + CSA + propagate_add → deskew_reg. The propagate add is still on the critical path!

For column N-1, the path is: multiply + CSA + propagate_add → c_data (combinational output, no register).

So in both cases, the propagate add is on the critical path. The only difference is whether it feeds a register or the output.

To truly remove the propagate add from the critical path, I need to register it separately. But that adds 1 cycle, which I can only compensate for columns 0..N-2 (by reducing deskew). For column N-1, I can't compensate.

What if column N-1 is NOT the critical path? If the propagate add for column N-1 (combinational, ~180ps) + multiply+CSA (~490ps) = 670ps is less than the path through columns 0..N-2 (where the propagate add is registered)?

For columns 0..N-2, the critical path is: multiply + CSA → deskew_reg (no propagate add in this path, since it's registered). This path is ~490ps. For column N-1, the path is: multiply + CSA + propagate_add → output, which is ~670ps. So column N-1 would be the critical path at 670ps.

That's no improvement. The propagate add is still on the critical path for column N-1.

Unless... I make column N-1 have 1 deskew stage too. How? By adding 1 to the input skew for all rows, which makes column N-1's total 2N (too much). Unless I also subtract 1 from the psum path.

What if I make the psum path 1 cycle shorter? Currently the psum path from PE(0,j) to PE(N-1,j) takes N-1 cycles. If I make the first PE's psum path 0 cycles (combinational), the total psum path is N-2 cycles. Then:
- Column j (j < N-1): total = i + j + (N-2-i) + 2 (bottom PE) + deskew = i + j + N - i + deskew = j + N + deskew. For 2N-1: deskew = N-1-j. Same deskew!
- Column N-1: total = i + (N-1) + (N-2-i) + 2 + 0 = 2N-1. With reg = 2N. ✗

Still 1 too many for column N-1.

What if I make the first PE take 0 cycles (combinational psum) and the last PE take 2 cycles (registered propagate add)?
- Total psum path: 0 (first PE, combinational) + (N-2)*1 (middle PEs) + 2 (last PE) = N.
- For column j: i + j + N + deskew. For 2N-1: deskew = N-1-j.
- Column N-1: i + (N-1) + N + 0 = 2N-1+i. This varies with i! Not good.

The psum path can't be combinational because it needs to be registered for the systolic timing.

I'm stuck on this approach. Let me try a completely different angle.

**What if I reduce the multiply delay by using a different multiplier architecture?** Let me try a Wallace tree multiplier manually, or try using Yosys's tech mapping differently.

Actually, let me try something simple: what if I compute the product using unsigned multiplication and handle the sign separately? The sign correction is just a conditional negate, which might be faster than a signed multiply tree.

For signed a × w:
- Compute unsigned |a| × |w| (8-bit × 8-bit unsigned = 16-bit)
- If sign(a) XOR sign(w), negate the result

But computing |a| requires a 2's complement (add 1 and invert), and the conditional negate is a 16-bit add/subtract. This might not be faster.

Actually, let me try the Baugh-Wooley approach more carefully. The key idea: for a N×N signed multiply, the Baugh-Wooley method modifies the partial products so that all additions are positive. Specifically:
- For bits i < N-1, j < N-1: pp = a[i] & w[j]
- For i = N-1 (sign bit of a), j < N-1: pp = ~a[N-1] & w[j] (complemented)
- For i < N-1, j = N-1 (sign bit of w): pp = a[i] & ~w[N-1] (complemented)  
- For i = N-1, j = N-1: pp = a[N-1] & w[N-1] (not complemented)
- Add a correction constant of 2^(2N-1) + 2^(N-1)

Wait, this is getting complicated. Let me try a different approach.

Let me try using a **precomputed weight lookup**. During weight load, precompute w * 0, w * 1, ..., w * 255 (or w * -128, ..., w * 127). Then during compute, the multiply becomes a table lookup. The table has 256 entries × 16 bits = 4096 bits per PE. That's 64 × 4096 = 262K bits total. Too much area!

But what about a smaller table? If I split the 8-bit activation into two 4-bit nibbles, each needs 16 entries × 12 bits = 192 bits per PE. Total: 64 × 2 × 192 = 24,576 bits. Still a lot, but let me check if the area is less than the current multiply.

Actually, the current multiply area (8×8 signed multiplier) is probably around 300-400 gates per PE. A 16-entry × 12-bit LUT is about 16 × 12 = 192 flip-flops + a 16:1 mux per bit = 12 × (16:1 mux) ≈ 12 × 8 = 96 gates. So 192 FFs + 96 gates = roughly 288 gates per LUT, × 2 = 576 gates. Plus the adder to combine. That's probably more than the current multiply.

Let me try a different approach: just try to help Yosys by using `(* mul_nopartial*)` or other attributes. Actually, let me check if there's a way to hint Yosys to use a specific multiplier architecture.

Let me try a completely different strategy: **reduce area instead of delay**. The delay is stuck at ~664ps. If I can reduce area while keeping the same delay, the cost goes down.

Area breakdown estimate:
- 64 PEs: each has 8-bit weight reg, 8-bit activation reg, 2× 19-bit carry-save regs = 8+8+38 = 54 FFs + multiply logic + CSA logic
- Skew: 28 8-bit regs = 224 FFs
- Deskew: 28 32-bit regs = 896 FFs (in design_cs_reg, first stage is 32-bit)
- Valid: 15 FFs

The deskew has 28×32 = 896 FFs. If I reduce this to 28×19 = 532 FFs (using 19-bit deskew with sign extension), I save 364 FFs. But I tried this and the delay went up. Let me try again with a different approach: do the propagate add in 19 bits, register in 19 bits, and sign-extend at the very end (combinationally):
  -> create_file(content="// Best design so far: design_cs_reg with AW=19\n// Try: 19-bit deskew with sign extension at output, registered propagate add\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter AW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [AW-1:0]\t );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   wire [AW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(AW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n\n   wire [AW-1:0] cs_sum =            psum_south_c <= {cs_carry[AW-2:0], 1'b0};\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter AW = 19)\n   (\n generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign c_bot_c[j*AW +: AW] = psum_c_v[N][j];\n      end\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .AW(AW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .rst        (rst),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_north_s (psum_s_v[i][j]),\n               .psum_north_c (psum_c_v[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_south_s (psum_s_v[i+1][j]),\n               .psum_south_c (psum_c_v[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter AW = 19,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n {{(ACCW-AW){prop[AW-1]}}, prop};\n         end\n         else begin : g_deskewn\n            // D stages of 19-bit registers\n            // Stage 0: registered propagate add\n            // Stages   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];\n\nendmodule\n", filename='design_cs_best.sv')

The 19-bit deskew is worse (2,947,387 vs 2,881,138). The 19-bit propagate add is apparently slower than the 32-bit one in Yosys's mapping. The 32-bit propagate add might use a more optimized structure.

always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= {DW{1'b0}};\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  sr[0] <= din;\n                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= sr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: [ASSISTANT]
The 18-bit carry-save has issues with the propagate add sign extension. The problem is my sign extension of the carry-save values to 19 bits for the propagate add. The issue is that carry-save values can't be sign-extended independently - the sign of the *result* depends on both s and c.

The correct way to handle 18-bit carry-save → 19-bit propagate add: extend both s and c by 1 bit (sign extension), then add. But sign-extending the carry is wrong because carry isn't a signed number in isolation.

The correct approach: since `s + c` represents the true value, and the true value fits in 19 bits, I should sign-extend `s` by 1 bit and extend `c` by 1 bit (same sign as the MSB of the true value, which is `s[17] ^ c[17]` ... no, that's not right either).

Actually, for carry-save: the true value is `s + c`. The carry `c` has its MSB at position 18 (since carry is shifted left by 1). So `c` is effectively a 18-bit number with bit 0 = 0. The sum `s + c` where both are 18 bits can be 19 bits. The correct extension:
- s_ext[18] = s[17] (sign extension of sum)
- c_ext[18] = c[17] (sign extension of carry, treating carry as a signed number)

But `c` is NOT a signed number - it's the carry vector. Its sign doesn't represent anything. The issue is that in carry-save, `c` is not independently meaningful.

The correct fix: make the carry-save AW=19 bits (which is what design_cs_reg does). The extra bit handles the carry propagation. Let me abandon the 18-bit approach.

Let me go back to the best design and try to optimize the multiplier. Let me try using an explicit Baugh-Wooley multiplier:
  -> create_file(content="// Baugh-Wooley signed multiplier for 8x8\n// All partial products are positive (no subtraction needed)\n// For a[7:0] * b[7:0] (both signed):\n// pp[i][j] = a[i] & b[j] for i<7, j<7\n// pp[7][j] = ~a[7] & b[j] for j<7 (complemented)\n// pp[i][7] = a[i] & ~b[7] for i<7 (complemented)\n// pp[7][7] = a[7] & b[7] (not complemented)\n// Add correction: 2^15 + 2^7 (i.e., bit 15 and bit 7 set)\n// Then sign-extend to AW\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter AW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [AW-1:0]\t  psum_north_s,\n    input [AW-1:0]\t  psum_north_c,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [AW-1:0] psum_south_s,\n    output reg [AW-1:0] psum_south_c\n   );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   // Baugh-Wooley partial products\n   // Row 0: a[0]*b[0], a[0]*~b[7], 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, a[0]*b[7] at bit 15\n   // Actually, let me just build it as an array of partial products\n   // and sum them using a carry-save tree.\n   \n   // For simplicity, let's build the product as sum of shifted partial products\n   // pp[i] = a_west[i] ? (w << i) : 0, with Baugh-Wooley sign handling\n   \n   // Actually, the simplest correct Baugh-Wooley:\n   // For i < 7: pp = a[i] ? (w << i) : 0\n   // For i = 7: pp = a[7] ? -(w << 7) : 0  (sign bit contributes negatively)\n   // But -(w << 7) = ~w << 7 + 2^7\n   // So: pp[7] = a[7] ? ((~w << 7) + 2^7) : 0\n   // Actually for Baugh-Wooley, we want all positive additions:\n   // a * w = sum_{i<7} a[i]*w*2^i + a[7]*(~w+1)*2^7\n   //       = sum_{i<7} a[i]*w*2^i + a[7]*~w*2^7 + a[7]*2^7\n   // But a[7]*~w*2^7 is still a positive partial product (if we consider ~w as unsigned)\n   // And a[7]*2^7 is a correction constant.\n   \n   // This is getting complex. Let me just use $signed multiply but \n   // try to reduce the number of partial products by splitting differently.\n   \n   // Actually, let me try: compute product as sum of two 4x8 multiplies\n   // a = {a_hi[3:0], a_lo[3:0]} (unsigned nibbles)\n   // a * w (signed) = (a_hi * w_signed * 16) + (a_lo * w_signed)\n   // But a_hi needs sign handling...\n   \n   // For signed a: a = -a[7]*128 + a[6:0]\n   // a * w = -a[7]*w*128 + a[6:0]*w\n   // a[6:0] is unsigned (0-127), w is signed\n   // a[6:0]*w is 7-bit * 8-bit signed = 14-bit + sign = 15-bit\n   // a[7]*w*128: if a[7]=1, subtract w*128\n   \n   // So: prod = a6_0 * w - (a7 ? (w << 7) : 0)\n   // a6_0 * w: unsigned 7-bit * signed 8-bit\n   // This can be done as: $signed({1'b0, a_west[6:0]}) * $signed(w)\n   \n   wire signed [14:0] prod_lo = $signed({1'b0, a_west[6:0]}) * $signed(w);\n   wire signed [15:0] prod_hi = a_west[7] ? -$signed(w) : 16'sd0;\n   // prod = prod_lo + (prod_hi << 7)\n   wire [AW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(AW-16){prod_hi[15]}}, prod_hi, 7'b0000000} +\n                            {{(AW-15){prod_lo[14]}}, prod_lo};\n\n   wire [AW-1:0] cs_sum = psum_north_s ^ psum_north_c ^ prod_ext;\n   wire [AW-1:0] cs_carry = (psum_north_s & psum_north_c) |\n                              (psum_north_s & prod_ext) |\n                              (psum_north_c & prod_ext);\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_south_s <= {AW{1'b0}};\n         psum_south_c <= {AW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east     <= a_west;\n            psum_south_s <= cs_sum;\n            psum_south_c <= {cs_carry[AW-2:0], 1'b0};\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter AW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*AW-1:0]\tc_bot_s,\n    output [N*AW-1:0]\tc_bot_c\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [AW-1:0] psum_s_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [AW-1:0] psum_c_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; [ASSISTANT]
The split multiply (7-bit unsigned × 8-bit signed + conditional subtract) is worse: delay 753ps (from 664ps), area 4194 (from 4338). The area is less but the delay is much worse. The split creates two multiplies + an add + a conditional, which is more complex.

Let me go back to design_cs_reg and try a different approach. Let me try using **`(* mul_map *)`** or other Yosys attributes to control multiplier implementation. Actually, let me try if Yosys has a `(* techmap_macc *)` or similar.

Actually, let me try a completely different optimization: **reduce the area of the skew chain**. The skew chain has 28 8-bit registers (0+1+2+3+4+5+6+7). These are shift registers. Can I reduce them?

One idea: use a single counter + mux instead of separate shift registers. But that would add a comparator and mux per row, which might not save area.

Another idea: **merge the skew registers into the PE array**. Currently, row i's activation is delayed by i cycles before entering the array. But PE(i,0) already has a register for a_east. If the first column of PEs could also serve as the skew registers... but the skew is applied before column 0, and the PEs in column 0 register a_east which goes to column 1.

Actually, the skew for row i is implemented as i registers before PE(i,0). PE(i,0) then registers a_west into a_east. So the total delay for row i is: i (skew) + 1 (PE(0) register) + ... + j (PE(j) register). The skew registers and the PE registers are separate.

Can I move the skew into the first column of PEs? For row i, instead of i skew registers before PE(i,0), have PE(i,0) take i extra cycles. But PE(i,0) is shared across all rows, so it can't have different delays for different rows.

Wait, each row has its own PEs. PE(i,0) is specific to row i. So PE(i,0) could have i extra registers for the activation. But PE(i,0) already registers a_west into a_east. If I add (i-1) more registers inside PE(i,0) for the activation path, that replaces the skew registers.

But this doesn't save any area - it's the same number of registers, just placed inside the PE instead of outside.

Let me try yet another approach: **use a smaller accumulator width (AW=17)**. Wait, I calculated that AW=19 is the minimum. But what if the test vectors don't actually hit the maximum? Let me check if AW=18 or even 17 would work for the test vectors.

Actually, the spec says "extreme tiles" are tested, so they likely include -128 * -128 = 16384, and 8 of those = 131072. AW must be ≥ 19.

Let me try to focus on reducing the delay. The multiply is the bottleneck. Let me try computing the product using a LUT precomputed during weight load:

The idea: during weight load, precompute `w * x` for x = 0..15 (4-bit lookup). Then the 8-bit activation is split into two 4-bit nibbles, and the product is `(lut_hi[a[7:4]] << 4) + lut_lo[a[3:0]]`. Each LUT has 16 entries × 12 bits = 192 bits. The lookup is just a 16:1 mux.

But wait - for signed multiply, the high nibble needs sign handling. Let me think...

a (signed 8-bit) = a[7]*(-128) + a[6:0] (unsigned 7-bit)
a * w = -a[7]*w*128 + a[6:0]*w

If I split into nibbles: a = a_hi*16 + a_lo where a_hi = a[7:4] (4-bit), a_lo = a[3:0] (4-bit)
a is signed, so a = (a_hi_signed)*16 + a_lo, where a_hi_signed = $signed(a[7:4]) (range -8 to 7)
a * w = a_hi_signed * w * 16 + a_lo * w

a_hi_signed * w: 4-bit signed × 8-bit signed = 12-bit signed
a_lo * w: 4-bit unsigned × 8-bit signed = 12-bit signed

I can precompute:
- LUT_hi[a_hi] = $signed(a_hi) * w (12-bit signed, for a_hi = 0..15, but $signed of 4-bit gives -8..7)
  - For a_hi = 8..15 (MSB set), $signed gives -8..-1
  - LUT entry is 12-bit signed
- LUT_lo[a_lo] = a_lo * w (12-bit, unsigned a_lo × signed w → 12-bit signed)
  - LUT entry is 12-bit signed

prod = (LUT_hi << 4) + LUT_lo

The critical path: a_west_reg → LUT read (16:1 mux) → shift + add → CSA. The LUT read is a 16:1 mux of 12-bit values, which is about 4 levels of 2:1 muxes. Then shift (free) + 12-bit add + CSA. The 12-bit add is smaller than the 8×8 multiply.

But the LUTs add 2 × 16 × 12 = 384 FFs per PE, × 64 PEs = 24,576 FFs. That's a lot of area. The current multiply uses maybe 300 gates per PE, while the LUTs use 384 FFs + 12 muxes per PE. Probably more area.

Let me try a different approach. Let me go back to the best design and try to find the optimal target_delay. Let me also try a moderate target_delay that might give a better area/delay tradeoff:
  -> run_evaluation(filename='design_cs_reg.sv', target_delay=550)

Let me try one more thing: **reduce the number of partial products in the multiply by using a_7 as a sign bit**. The multiply becomes:
- For bits 0-6: partial product = a[i] & w (7 partial products)
- For bit 7 (sign): subtract a[7] & w << 7

The subtraction is: -a[7]*w*2^7. In the CSA, this can be handled as adding ~w*2^7 + 2^7 (two's complement negation). The correction constant 2^7 can be absorbed into the sign extension.

This reduces partial products from 8 to 7 (one less level in the carry-save tree). But adds the negation logic. Let me try:

Let me try one more bold idea: **what if I remove the CSA and go back to the original propagate-add PE, but use a smaller accumulator width (AW=19)?** The original design had delay 1004ps with 32-bit accumulate. With 19-bit accumulate, the propagate add should be faster:
  -> create_file(content="// Original PE structure (propagate add) but with AW=19 accumulator\n// This reduces the adder width from 32 to 19\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter AW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [AW-1:0]\t  psum_north,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [AW-1:0] psum_south\n   );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   wire [AW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(AW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_south <= {AW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east     <= a_west;\n            psum_south <= psum_north + prod_ext;\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter AW = 19,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [AW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_v[0][j] = {AW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         wire [AW-1:0] result = psum_v[N][j];\n         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = {{(ACCW-AW){result[AW-1]}}, result};\n      end\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .AW(AW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .rst        (rst),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_north (psum_v[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_south (psum_v[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter AW = 19,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew\n         wire [DW-1:0] din =\n\t\t       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;\n         end\n         else begin : g_skewn\n            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= {DW{1'b0}};\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  sr[0] <= din;\n                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= sr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = sr[i-1];\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   wire [N*ACCW-1:0] c_bot;\n   tpu_array #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .AW(AW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_array\n     (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .ld(ld), .en(en),\n      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), .c_bot(c_bot));\n\n   genvar j;\n   generate\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_deskew\n         localparam D = N - 1 - j;\n         wire [ACCW-1:0] cin = c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW];\n         if (D == 0) begin : g_deskew0\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = cin;\n         end\n         else begin : g_deskewn\n            reg [ACCW-1:0] dr [0:D-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; k < D; k = k + 1)\n                    dr[k] <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  dr[0] <= cin;\n                  for (k = 1; k < D; k = k + 1)\n                    dr[k] <= dr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = dr[D-1];\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   reg [LAT-1:0] vpipe;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst)\n        vpipe <= {LAT{1'b0}};\n      else if (en)\n        vpipe <= {vpipe[LAT-2:0], a_valid};\n   end\n   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];\n\nendmodule\n", filename='design_prop19.sv')

## Summary

### 1. Approaches Tried

| Design | Cost | Delay (ps) | Area | Key idea |
|--------|------|-----------|------|----------|
| `design.sv` (baseline) | 4,624,408 | 1004 | 4604 | Original systolic array, 32-bit propagate-add psum chain |
| `design_cs.sv` | 3,104,542 | 679 | 4568 | **Carry-save accumulation** through psum chain, final propagate add at column bottom |
| `design_cs19.sv` | 2,893,914 | 667 | 4339 | CS + **19-bit truncated accumulator** (vs 32-bit), sign-extend at output |
| `design_cs_reg.sv` ★best | 2,881,138 | 664 | 4338 | CS + 19-bit + **registered propagate add** as first deskew stage for cols 0–6, combinational for col 7 |
| `design_prop19.sv` | 3,197,314 | 810 | 3944 | Propagate-add PE with 19-bit accumulator (no CS) — less area but much worse delay |

### 2. Most Impactful Optimizations

1. **Carry-save accumulation** (−33% cost): Replacing the per-PE propagate add with a 3:2 CSA eliminated the ripple-carry chain. The CSA is just XOR + MAJ gates (~70ps) vs a 32-bit propagate add (~300ps). Only one final propagate add per column at the bottom.

2. **19-bit truncated accumulator** (−7% cost): The max accumulated value is 8×128×128 = 131072 = 2¹⁷, requiring 19-bit signed. Reducing from 32→19 bits saved register area (64×2×13 = 1664 fewer FF bits) and reduced CSA/propagate-add width.

3. **Registered propagate add** (−0.4% cost): Moving the column-bottom propagate add into the first deskew register stage split the multiply+CSA+add path for columns 0–6.

### 3. What Didn't Work / Regressions

- **Booth radix-4 multiplier** (`design_booth.sv`): Sign handling bugs; abandoned. The partial product sign extension for the MSB group was wrong.
- **Split multiply** (7-bit unsigned × 8-bit + conditional subtract, `design_bw.sv`): Two smaller multiplies + add was slower (753ps vs 664ps).
- **18-bit carry-save** (`design_cs18.sv`): Carry-save values can't be independently sign-extended — the propagate add sign extension was wrong.
- **19-bit deskew registers** (`design_cs19b.sv`, `design_cs_best.sv`): Yosys mapped the narrower 19-bit propagate add *slower* than the 32-bit version, increasing delay (680ps vs 664ps).
- **Carry-save deskew** (`design_cs_full.sv`): Doubled deskew register count (2×19 vs 32 bits), increasing area.
- **Bottom-PE registered propagate add** (`design_cs_bot.sv`): Added 1 extra cycle that couldn't be compensated for column N-1 (deskew=0), breaking cycle accuracy.

### 4. Lessons Learned

- **Carry-save is the single biggest win** for MAC-chain critical paths — it transforms N sequential additions into N parallel CSAs + 1 final propagate add.
- **Yosys can't speed up an 8×8 multiply below ~420ps** regardless of target_delay — the multiply partial product tree is the floor.
- **Sign extension of carry-save pairs is non-trivial** — `s` and `c` can't be independently sign-extended; both must be the same width and the propagate add must produce the correct sign.
- **Column N-1 (deskew=0) is the fundamental constraint** — any extra registered stage can't be compensated since its deskew is already 0.
- **Narrower adders aren't always faster in Yosys** — the 19-bit propagate add mapped slower than 32-bit, likely due to different cell selection.
- Would try next: a correct Booth radix-4 implementation (halving partial products), or a precomputed weight LUT during load phase to replace the runtime multiplier.
